# Acer Session Handoff - Toolkit Sync (Build 5b)

## One-Line Answer
==The Dell's WSL toolkit was upgraded and its configs were turned into one golden set in this vault; the Acer must apply the same set so both laptops behave identically, and several things on the Acer are missing or wrong and need fixing there.==

## Who this is for
The Claude session running on the Acer (Windows plus WSL Ubuntu-24.04, user `anant_gupta`). You have no access to the Dell. Everything you need is in this vault, which Syncthing carries to you. Wait for Syncthing to finish before starting, then check the golden folder exists.

Vault root as the Acer sees it from WSL: `/mnt/d/_Anant/20_Progress/Documents/Jarvis`. Shorthand used below: `$P` = `<vault>/40_Resources/CS/Concepts/New Laptop/Old Laptop Rebuild/WSL Parity`.
```
V=/mnt/d/_Anant/20_Progress/Documents/Jarvis
P="$V/40_Resources/CS/Concepts/New Laptop/Old Laptop Rebuild/WSL Parity"
ls "$P/golden/home" "$P/_scripts"
```
If the path does not exist, find the vault with `ls /mnt/d/_Anant/*/Documents` and adjust; the scripts accept `--vault <path>`.

Same visit (added 2026-10-15): also run Part 0 of [[Acer Run Sheet - Windows Toolkit]] and the Build 2 SSH items in [[Old Laptop Rebuild - Build 2 Acer Handoff]] (host-key comparison, sync-check over SSH, large Git transfer, clone inventory, optional WSL route test).

## Rules for this session
- Read-only first; every script has `--dry-run`. Back up before editing (the scripts do it for you).
- Never print `.bashrc` broadly, `~/.mcp.env`, tokens, keys or auth files. Never copy secrets into the vault.
- Do not edit files under `golden/` on the Acer. If a golden file is wrong for the Acer, write the reason under Results below and let the Dell session change the golden copy, so the two laptops never diverge.
- No `wsl --shutdown` from inside WSL. Host steps go in a script for the user.
- No em dashes in vault notes.

## Steps (in order)
1. Health baseline, read-only. Install the checker, run it, keep the output for the Results section.
   `install -m 0755 "$P/_scripts/36-wsl-check.sh" ~/.local/bin/wsl-check.sh && wsl-check.sh`
2. Close any tmux server before touching tmux or its config: `tmux ls` should say no server (or `tmux kill-server`; continuum restores sessions later).
3. Apply the golden configs. Dry run, read the list, then apply.
   `bash "$P/_scripts/37-toolkit-apply.sh" --dry-run`
   `bash "$P/_scripts/37-toolkit-apply.sh"`
   It backs up every file it changes to `~/.config/.toolkit-backups/<timestamp>/`, scans for secrets first, builds the bat theme, wires git to the shared include, removes the no-op `core.fsmonitor`, and appends ONE guarded line to `.bashrc`.
4. Upgrade the binaries (user space, pinned, checksum-verified, reversible).
   `bash "$P/_scripts/38-toolkit-upgrade.sh" --dry-run`
   `bash "$P/_scripts/38-toolkit-upgrade.sh"`
   Rollback any tool: `bash "$P/_scripts/38-toolkit-upgrade.sh" --rollback <tool>`. The default set now ALSO pins pnpm to 12.4.2 (Corepack) and builds tmux 3.8 from source into `~/.local/bin` (static libevent, a no-op yacc stub because the tarball ships `cmd-parse.c`, no sudo). tmux is skipped while a tmux server runs: close every tmux session on the Acer first (`tmux kill-server`), otherwise rerun later with `--only tmux`. The Dell is already on tmux 3.8 and pnpm 12.4.2.
5. Reload tmux plugins: start tmux, press `Ctrl-a` then `I` (capital i) once so TPM installs any missing plugin.
6. Fix the Acer-only problems in the table below.
7. Verify and report: `wsl-check.sh`, `ya pkg list`, `starship explain | head`, `bat --list-themes | grep tokyonight_storm`, `git config --show-origin --get core.pager`. Paste the results in the Results section.

## What is missing or wrong on the Acer (evidence, then fix)
| # | Problem | Evidence | Fix |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `core.autocrlf=false` in WSL silently commits CRLF; Build 2 git keys absent | Acer snapshot `.gitconfig` | Step 3 adds the shared include (pull.rebase, push.autoSetupRemote, fetch.prune, rerere). Then `git config --global core.autocrlf input`. |
| 2 | `core.fsmonitor=true` and `untrackedCache` copied from the Windows config; fsmonitor does nothing in WSL (`git fsmonitor--daemon status` says unsupported on this platform) | Dell test on git 2.43 | Step 3 removes `core.fsmonitor`; `untrackedCache` stays in the shared file. |
| 3 | `starship.toml` in WSL has CRLF line endings | snapshot copy 2,379 B vs 2,260 B LF; hash equals the Windows file after LF normalisation | Step 3 overwrites with the LF golden file. Keep Windows and WSL starship files identical after LF normalisation. |
| 4 | starship format lists no `$python` or `$conda`, yet conda `changeps1` is off "because Starship shows the env" | `Installations` note and snapshot format string | Golden adds both segments (step 3). |
| 5 | `.tmux.conf`: splits lose the current directory, no `history-limit`, no `renumber-windows`, `escape-time` left at the tmux 3.4 default of 500 ms, no truecolor or passthrough settings, comments still say WezTerm | snapshot `.tmux.conf` | Golden `.tmux.conf` (step 3). Restart tmux afterwards. |
| 6 | gh-dash `repoPaths` points `NousResearch/hermes-agent` at `/mnt/d/Apps/Hermes/hermes-agent` (a Windows path) | snapshot `gh-dash/config.yml` | Golden has `repoPaths: {}`. Add Acer-only entries by hand afterwards, in the form `owner/repo: ~/projects/<group>/<repo>` with ext4 paths. Re-running step 3 resets the file (a backup is kept). |
| 7 | `.bashrc` JARVIS block: every hook unguarded (atuin env, starship, zoxide, direnv), `PATH` prepended again on every re-source, nvm loaded eagerly (slow startup), `~/.mcp.env` sourced into every interactive shell so its secrets reach every process | snapshot `.bashrc.jarvis_block` | Optional hardening, do it with a backup and without printing the file: guard each hook with `command -v <tool> >/dev/null &&`, prepend `~/.local/bin` only if absent, source `.mcp.env` only from the tools that need it. Update delta, atuin, uv with their own installers; update claude with its own updater. |

| 11 | `.wslconfig` in the snapshot says 16GB / 8 CPUs with no swap line; notes say it was corrected to 20GB / 18 CPUs / swap on D: | `Ubuntu - WSL` note, 2026-10-12 correction | In Windows PowerShell: `Get-Content $env:USERPROFILE\.wslconfig`; in WSL: `free -h; nproc; cat /proc/swaps`. Report real values. Do not change it from inside WSL. |

Report `pnpm --version` (expect 12.4.2) and which shared repos have `packageManager` in `package.json`; adding `pnpm@12.4.2` there is a per-repo approval. |
| 13 | `tmux-handlr` ntfy topic never confirmed | `Acer Live State` round 2 | Check `~/.tmux/plugins/tmux-handlr` config for a topic; if none, tell the user (the topic is secret-like, keep it out of the vault). |
| 14 | `snapshot excluded-wsl.txt` has a junk first line ("Shell cwd was reset") | snapshot | Harmless. Ignore. |
| 15 | tmux 3.7c vs Dell 3.4 | `VERSIONS.md` | DECIDED: both on 3.8. The Dell is on 3.8 already (2026-10-14). Step 4 builds 3.8 on the Acer (close tmux first). Expect `tmux -V` = `tmux 3.8`. |

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
- The Dell was rebuilt in layers. Build 1 covered WSL, Build 2 the SSH workflow, Build 3 C: cleanup, Build 4 the Windows toolkit, Build 4b the leftovers, and Build 5 WSL parity. Each layer left a prompt and a findings note, and the Acer handoff is the one piece still open.
- The notes follow a consistent method. Scripts are dry-run first, back up before changing anything, and quarantine instead of deleting. Every note ends with Growth rules (a standing rule plus one check command).

`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

## Where things stand
- **Dell:** Builds 1 to 5 and 4b are closed. WSL 3.0.3.0 runs with zero failed units, C: has about 138 GiB free, and the golden configs plus tmux 3.8 and pnpm 12.4.2 are applied. The WSL export is verified by tar listing and checksum, but no import test was run.
- **Still open on the Dell:**
  - Run `07-build5-leftovers.ps1 -Apply`.
  - Revoke the Firecrawl key, which is still accepted.
  - Pin `packageManager` in repos, one repo at a time.
  - Decide on Malwarebytes, WhatsApp, Rize and GitKrakenCLI.
- **Waiting on the Acer:**
  - The Build 5b handoff and the Windows toolkit run sheet.
  - The Build 2 SSH items: host-key comparison, `sync-check` over SSH, the large Git transfer, and the clone inventory.
- **Declined on 2026-10-15:** the port-2222 sshd fallback and the VS Code Tunnel.
- **Later builds:** 6, 7 and 8 have no prompts yet.

## Where the notes disagree with the handoff
1. **The Acer's `~/.cache/node` will be deleted.** The Acer's Sunday 03:00 `weekly-cache-cleanup.timer` deletes `~/.cache/node`, which holds Corepack's pnpm pin. The same bug moved the Dell's pnpm from 10.33.2 to 12.11.2. Step 4 pins pnpm 12.4.2, and the timer would undo it. The Acer audit proposes fixing the timer first.
2. **Table row 10 is partly done.** The Acer already has chafa 1.18.2, direnv 2.32.1 and Miniconda with `jupyter-base`. The audit says not to apply the whole fix list blindly.
3. **tmux "3.7c vs Dell 3.4" is stale.** The Dell is on 3.8.
4. **The Acer's Linux disk is small.** Its filesystem is 40 GiB and about 70% used (about 10.9 GiB free). Building tmux from source and the step 4 downloads are tight on that.
5. **The Acer's `.wslconfig` isn't active yet.** The saved file says 20GB, 18 CPUs and 8GB swap. The running VM still has about 15.6 GiB, 8 CPUs and 4 GiB swap, because it hasn't had a cold restart. The `sparseVhd` and `autoMemoryReclaim` keys also sit under `[wsl2]` instead of `[experimental]`.
6. **pnpm is not one value across machines.** WSL on both laptops is 12.4.2 through Corepack. The Dell's Windows copy is 12.11.2 from winget. The Acer's audit shows WSL at 12.11.2 because of item 1.

## Next step
Say which part you want. I can fix the `weekly-cache-cleanup` timer first, since it undoes step 4's pnpm pin, or I can start the handoff from step 1 (the read-only `wsl-check.sh` baseline) and wait for your go-ahead before each change.

# Old Laptop Rebuild - Runs Index

Everything that has already been run for the Dell rebuild: the prompt that was sent and the findings it produced, one folder per run. Prompts that have not run yet, living reference notes and the roadmap stay one level up in [[Old Laptop Rebuild - Index]]. Reorganised 2026-10-13 and again 2026-10-14. Obsidian links use note names, so no link broke.

## Folder layout
| Folder | Holds |
|---|---|
| `Build 1 WSL/` | prompt and findings |
| `Build 2 VS Code and Two-Laptop/` | prompt and findings (Acer-side Phase 4 items are still open and live in the Index) |
| `Build 3 Windows Cleanup/` | the combined prompt note (Build 3 ran; its Build 4 half is superseded), the findings, and the 3b snapshot and watch note |
| `Build 4 Windows Toolkit/` | prompt and findings (Phases 1 to 4 ran; the Build 4b results are the last section of the findings note) |
| `Build 4b Dell Leftovers and Tailscale/` | the Build 4b prompt (its results live in the Build 4 findings note, section "Build 4b") |
| `Build 5 WSL and D Drive/` | prompt and findings (closed on the Dell 2026-10-15; only the Acer sync is open) |
| `_backups/` | every dated `.bak`, `.pre-*` and pre-merge copy, the two Index copies from the Windows and WSL sessions, and `sync-conflicts-20261013/` |
| this folder | [[Old Laptop Rebuild - Index History (to 2026-10-12)]] and this note |

## What each run did
| Run | Dates | Prompt | Findings | Result in one line |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Build 1, WSL layer | 2026-10-04 to 10-07 | [[Old Laptop Rebuild - Prompt 1 WSL]] | [[Old Laptop Rebuild - Build 1 WSL Findings]] | 36.4 GiB of project artifacts and 5.3 GiB of caches reclaimed in ext4; `.wslconfig` aligned for the always-on SSH host |
| Build 2, WSL VS Code and two-laptop SSH | 2026-10-07 to 10-12 | [[Old Laptop Rebuild - Prompt 2 VS Code and Two-Laptop Workflow]] | [[Old Laptop Rebuild - Build 2 VS Code and Two-Laptop Findings]] | Tailscale in WSL, direct path to the Acer, plain SSH to the canonical checkout proven; Acer-side items open |

## Two-laptop workflow (locked direction, 2026-10-04)
The Dell is the canonical host: one checkout per shared repo on its WSL ext4 disk. The Acer connects through VS Code Remote-SSH over Tailscale. Tailscale runs in exactly one place per machine: inside WSL on the Dell (the Tailscale SSH server is Linux/macOS only), and on the Windows side only on the Acer (Remote-SSH uses the Windows `ssh.exe`). Decided 2026-10-04. GitHub stays the backup and PR channel, parallel tasks use git worktrees with `<machine>/<topic>` branches, and no sync daemon touches code. Cost: the Dell must be awake and on the tailnet. Fallback when it is off: the Acer pushes a branch from its own clone. Reasoning: a single copy cannot drift, which is the property the Jarvis sync could not give without constant repair ([[Cross-Laptop Sync - Known Failure Modes and Prevention]]). Network exposure (Tailscale, sshd) needs the user's approval inside the session.

## Settings Sync is live on the Dell
The Acer's cloud copy has landed on the Dell's Windows VS Code, with Acer-only paths in the synced `settings.json`. Anything written to a Windows user-level VS Code file on the Dell reaches the Acer. Build 2 stays out of those files and records findings for the Windows-side build.

## Findings that change the plan
- **There is no discrete GPU.** "Use the GPU" means the Iris Xe only. CUDA, NVIDIA container tooling and GPU-passthrough tuning do not apply. Heavy local-model work belongs on the Acer.
- **The pagefile warning has a visible cause.** Windows reports `D:\pagefile.sys` as the configured pagefile (system-managed, `AutomaticManagedPagefile` off), but no pagefile exists on D:. The live one is a 25.6 GB `C:\pagefile.sys`. The likely story is that Windows cannot create the D: file at boot, falls back to a temporary C: file, and shows the "pagefile" pop-up. This is a hypothesis for the Windows-host session to confirm in the event log. The fix is an explicit, sized pagefile configuration, not another reboot.
- **WSL swap defaults to C:.** No `swapfile=` setting exists, the same bug the Acer notes record.
- **Jarvis MCP needs mirrored networking.** From WSL, `127.0.0.1:27123` is Obsidian on Windows only in mirrored mode ([[VS Code - MCP and Secrets]]). The statement in [[Ubuntu - WSL]] that NAT alone forwards localhost both ways is only true from Windows to WSL, so the Dell keeps mirrored.
- **A past quarantine sits on C:.** `[REDACTED]` (2.3 GB) is on the drive being freed.
- **Running `wsl --shutdown` from inside a WSL session kills the session.** Host steps go in a script the user runs.

## Logging rule for every session
Each session writes `Old Laptop Rebuild - Build N <layer> Findings.md` here, with a before/after size table, every state-changing command, the root cause of every error, and a Growth rules section (the standing rule and one check command per thing that grew).

`wsl-check config` now reports 9 differences against the old September snapshot. These are the golden set deliberately replacing the old files: starship LF, tmux, atuin, lazygit, sesh, yazi, git ignore, gh-dash and `.gitconfig`. They are not drift.

#!/usr/bin/env bash
# 36-wsl-check.sh (Build 5): read-only health check for the Dell WSL host. One section per thing that can drift or grow.
# Usage: 36-wsl-check.sh [config|tools|disk|swap|editors|backup|dtree|all]   (default: all)
set -uo pipefail
export PATH="$HOME/.local/bin:$HOME/.cargo/bin:$HOME/miniconda3/bin:/usr/local/bin:/usr/bin:/bin:$PATH"
SNAP="/mnt/d/Users/_Anant/10_Areas/Documents/Jarvis/40_Resources/CS/Concepts/New Laptop/Old Laptop Rebuild/Acer Dotfile Snapshot"
what="${1:-all}"
want() { [ "$what" = all ] || [ "$what" = "$1" ]; }

if want config; then
  echo "== config hashes vs the Acer snapshot (MATCH or MATCH-LF expected; gh-dash, .gitconfig, wsl.conf differ on purpose)"
  tail -n +2 "$SNAP/hashes-wsl.txt" | while IFS=$'\t' read -r rel _src sh ch _sz _st; do
    [ "$sh" = n/a ] && continue
    case "$rel" in wsl/etc/*) live=/etc/${rel#wsl/etc/};; wsl/home/*) live=$HOME/${rel#wsl/home/};; *) continue;; esac
    [ -e "$live" ] || { echo "MISSING   $rel"; continue; }
    lh=$(sha256sum "$live" | cut -d' ' -f1)
    ln=$(tr -d '\r' < "$live" | sha256sum | cut -d' ' -f1); sn=$(tr -d '\r' < "$SNAP/$rel" | sha256sum | cut -d' ' -f1)
    if [ "$lh" = "$ch" ]; then r=MATCH; elif [ "$ln" = "$sn" ]; then r=MATCH-LF; else r=DIFF; fi
    echo "$r $rel"
  done | sort | awk '{c[$1]++; if($1=="DIFF"||$1=="MISSING") d=d "\n    " $2} END{for(k in c) print "  " k ": " c[k]; if(d!="") print "  not matching:" d}'
fi
if want tools; then
  echo "== tool versions"
  for t in node pnpm uv ruff shfmt shellcheck conda claude codex tmux delta starship ya lazygit atuin sesh socat bwrap; do
    if ! command -v "$t" >/dev/null 2>&1; then printf '  %-11s MISSING\n' "$t"; continue; fi
    case "$t" in
      tmux) v=$(tmux -V) ;;
      lazygit) v=$(lazygit --version 2>&1 | grep -o 'version=[0-9.]*') ;;
      shellcheck) v=$(shellcheck --version | sed -n 2p) ;;
      socat) v=$(socat -V 2>&1 | sed -n 2p) ;;
      pnpm) v=$(COREPACK_ENABLE_DOWNLOAD_PROMPT=0 pnpm --version 2>&1 | tail -n 1) ;;
      *) v=$("$t" --version 2>&1 | head -n 1) ;;
    esac
    printf '  %-11s %s\n' "$t" "$(printf '%s' "$v" | cut -c1-60)"
  done
  echo "  systemd: $(systemctl is-system-running) ; failed units: $(systemctl --failed --no-legend | wc -l) ; ollama: $(systemctl is-enabled ollama 2>&1)"
fi
if want disk; then
  echo "== disk"; df -h / | tail -n 1; ls -l /mnt/d/WSL/Ubuntu/ext4.vhdx | awk '{printf "  ext4.vhdx %.1f GiB\n", $5/1073741824}'
  df -h /mnt/d /mnt/c | tail -n 2
  if [ -f "$HOME/.local/state/wsl-watch/ALERT" ]; then cat "$HOME/.local/state/wsl-watch/ALERT"; else echo "  watcher: no alert"; fi
fi
if want swap; then
  echo "== swap"; free -m | awk '/Swap/{print "  swap used " $3 " MB of " $2 " MB"}'; ls -l /mnt/d/WSL/swap.vhdx | awk '{printf "  swap.vhdx %.0f MB\n", $5/1048576}'
  grep -E '^(swap|swapfile|memory|processors)' /mnt/c/Users/*/.wslconfig 2>/dev/null | tr -d '\r' | sed 's/^/  /'
fi
if want editors; then
  echo "== editor servers and caches"; du -sh ~/.vscode-server ~/.cursor-server ~/.vscode-remote-containers ~/.npm/_npx ~/.cache ~/miniconda3 2>/dev/null | sed 's/^/  /'
  echo "  clean timer: $(systemctl --user is-active wsl-clean.timer) ; watch timer: $(systemctl --user is-active wsl-watch.timer)"
fi
if want backup; then
  echo "== tarball and export backup"
  ls -l /mnt/d/WSL/Installers/wsl-ubuntu.tar 2>&1 | awk '{print "  old tar: " $5 " bytes"}'
  ls /mnt/d/Backup/WSL/ubuntu-*.tar 2>/dev/null | sed 's/^/  export: /'
  ls -d /mnt/d/Quarantine/wsl-tar-review-* 2>/dev/null | sed 's/^/  quarantine: /'
fi
if want dtree; then
  echo "== D: tree and Drive"; ls /mnt/d/_Anant /mnt/d/Users/_Anant 2>&1 | sed 's/^/  /'
  powershell.exe -NoProfile -Command 'if(Get-Process GoogleDriveFS -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue){"  Drive running"}else{"  Drive not running"}; "  DriveFS dir GB: " + [math]::Round((Get-ChildItem "$env:LOCALAPPDATA\Google\DriveFS" -Recurse -Force -File -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue | Measure-Object Length -Sum).Sum/1GB,2)' 2>/dev/null | tr -d '\r\0'
fi
exit 0
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# 37-toolkit-apply.sh (Build 5b, 2026-10-13). Applies the GOLDEN toolkit configs to this WSL (Dell or Acer).
# Source: vault Old Laptop Rebuild/WSL Parity/golden/home (one copy of truth for both laptops).
# What it does, in order: secret scan -> copy every golden file with timestamped backups (CR stripped) -> bat theme build ->
#   git wiring (include ~/.config/git/shared.gitconfig, drop the keys now owned by it, drop no-op core.fsmonitor) ->
#   one guarded source line in ~/.bashrc (TOOLKIT_ENV_BLOCK). It never prints .bashrc and never touches secrets.
# Usage: 37-toolkit-apply.sh [--dry-run] [--vault PATH]     (on the Acer pass --vault if auto-detection fails)
# Exit code is non-zero when a required step fails. ASCII only, LF endings.
set -uo pipefail
DRY=0; VAULT_ARG=""
while [ $# -gt 0 ]; do
  case "$1" in
    --dry-run) DRY=1 ;;
    --vault) shift; VAULT_ARG="${1:-}" ;;
    --help|-h) sed -n '2,11p' "$0"; exit 0 ;;
    *) echo "unknown option: $1" >&2; exit 2 ;;
  esac; shift
done
NODEBIN=$(ls -d "$HOME"/.nvm/versions/node/*/bin 2>/dev/null | tail -n 1 || true)
export PATH="$HOME/.local/bin:$HOME/.cargo/bin:${NODEBIN:+$NODEBIN:}/usr/local/bin:/usr/bin:/bin:$PATH"

REL="40_Resources/CS/Concepts/New Laptop/Old Laptop Rebuild/WSL Parity/golden/home"
GOLD=""
for base in "$VAULT_ARG" "${VAULT:-}" "/mnt/d/Users/_Anant/10_Areas/Documents/Jarvis" "/mnt/d/_Anant/20_Progress/Documents/Jarvis" "/mnt/d/_Anant/10_Areas/Documents/Jarvis"; do
  if [ -n "$base" ] && [ -d "$base/$REL" ]; then GOLD="$base/$REL"; break; fi
done
[ -n "$GOLD" ] || { echo "FAIL: golden dir not found. Pass --vault <path to the Jarvis vault as seen from WSL>"; exit 1; }

TS=$(date +%Y%m%d-%H%M%S); BK="$HOME/.config/.toolkit-backups/$TS"
FAILS=0; CHANGED=0; SAME=0; TABLE=()
say()  { printf '%s\n' "$*"; }
fail() { say "FAIL: $*"; FAILS=$((FAILS+1)); }
sha()  { sha256sum "$1" | cut -d' ' -f1; }
PATTERNS=('AKIA[0-9A-Z]{16}' 'gh[pousr]_[A-Za-z0-9]{30,}' 'github_pat_[A-Za-z0-9_]{30,}' 'sk-[A-Za-z0-9]{20,}' 'xox[baprs]-[A-Za-z0-9-]{10,}'
  '-----BEGIN[ A-Z]*PRIVATE KEY-----' 'AIza[0-9A-Za-z_-]{35}' 'ya29\.[0-9A-Za-z_-]+'
  '(api[_-]?key|secret|passwd|password)[[:space:]]*[=:][[:space:]]*[^[:space:]#]{8,}' 'Bearer[[:space:]]+[A-Za-z0-9._-]{15,}')
has_secret() { local p; for p in "${PATTERNS[@]}"; do if grep -qiE "$p" "$1" 2>/dev/null; then say "  secret pattern hit in $1: $p"; return 0; fi; done; return 1; }
STAGE=$(mktemp -d); trap 'rm -rf "$STAGE"' EXIT
stage() { local src="$1" out; out="$STAGE/$(printf '%s' "$1" | sha256sum | cut -c1-16)"
  if [ "${src##*.}" != png ] && grep -q $'\r' "$src"; then tr -d '\r' < "$src" > "$out"; else cp "$src" "$out"; fi; printf '%s' "$out"; }
backup() { local rel="${1#$HOME/}"; mkdir -p "$BK/$(dirname "$rel")" && cp -p "$1" "$BK/$rel"; }
install_file() { # staged, dest, label
  local src="$1" dst="$2" label="$3" before="-" after; after=$(sha "$src"); [ -f "$dst" ] && before=$(sha "$dst")
  if [ "$before" = "$after" ]; then SAME=$((SAME+1)); TABLE+=("SAME	$label	$after	$after"); return 0; fi
  if [ "$DRY" = 1 ]; then say "  would write $label"; TABLE+=("WOULD-WRITE	$label	$before	$after"); CHANGED=$((CHANGED+1)); return 0; fi
  [ -f "$dst" ] && backup "$dst"
  mkdir -p "$(dirname "$dst")" && cp "$src" "$dst" && chmod 0644 "$dst" || { fail "write $dst"; return 1; }
  [ "$(sha "$dst")" = "$after" ] || { fail "read-back mismatch $dst"; return 1; }
  say "  wrote $label"; CHANGED=$((CHANGED+1)); TABLE+=("WRITTEN	$label	$before	$after")
}

say "== Build 5b toolkit apply ($TS) dry-run=$DRY"; say "   golden: $GOLD"
[ "$DRY" = 0 ] && mkdir -p "$BK"

say "== 1. secret scan"
n=0; while IFS= read -r -d '' f; do n=$((n+1)); if has_secret "$f"; then fail "secret scan: $f"; fi; done < <(find "$GOLD" -type f ! -name '*.png' -print0)
[ "$FAILS" -eq 0 ] || { say "== ABORT: secret scan failed, nothing copied"; exit 1; }
say "  $n files clean"

say "== 2. copy golden files"
while IFS= read -r -d '' f; do
  rel="${f#$GOLD/}"
  install_file "$(stage "$f")" "$HOME/$rel" "$rel"
done < <(find "$GOLD" -type f -print0 | sort -z)

say "== 3. bat theme (Tokyo Night Storm tmTheme from the Yazi flavor)"
TM="$HOME/.config/yazi/flavors/tokyonight-storm.yazi/tmtheme.xml"; BT="$HOME/.config/bat/themes/tokyonight_storm.tmTheme"
BATBIN=$(command -v bat || command -v batcat || true)
if [ -f "$TM" ] && [ -n "$BATBIN" ]; then
  if [ "$DRY" = 1 ]; then say "  would copy tmTheme and run: $BATBIN cache --build"
  else
    mkdir -p "$(dirname "$BT")" && cp "$TM" "$BT" && "$BATBIN" cache --build >/dev/null 2>&1
    if "$BATBIN" --list-themes 2>/dev/null | grep -q tokyonight_storm; then say "  bat theme tokyonight_storm registered"; else fail "bat theme build"; fi
  fi
else say "  skipped (yazi flavor or bat missing)"; fi

say "== 4. git wiring"
INC="$HOME/.config/git/shared.gitconfig"
if [ "$DRY" = 1 ]; then say "  would: add include.path, unset core.pager core.untrackedCache core.fsmonitor interactive.diffFilter delta.* merge.conflictstyle diff.colormoved from ~/.gitconfig"
else
  [ -f "$HOME/.gitconfig" ] && backup "$HOME/.gitconfig"
  if ! git config --global --get-all include.path | grep -qx "$INC"; then git config --global --add include.path "$INC" || fail "git include"; fi
  for k in core.pager core.untrackedCache core.fsmonitor interactive.diffFilter delta.navigate delta.side-by-side delta.line-numbers delta.dark merge.conflictstyle diff.colormoved; do
    git config --global --unset-all "$k" 2>/dev/null; done
  for kv in "core.pager=delta" "merge.conflictstyle=zdiff3" "diff.algorithm=histogram" "worktree.guessremote=true" "delta.dark=true"; do
    k="${kv%%=*}"; v="${kv#*=}"; [ "$(git config --get "$k")" = "$v" ] || fail "git $k expected $v got '$(git config --get "$k")'"; done
  say "  include wired; core.autocrlf=$(git config --global --get core.autocrlf) (machine-specific, left as is)"
fi

say "== 5. ~/.bashrc source line (backup first; file content is not printed)"
MARK="# >>> TOOLKIT_ENV_BLOCK (Build 5b) >>>"
if grep -qF "$MARK" "$HOME/.bashrc" 2>/dev/null; then say "  block already present"
elif [ "$DRY" = 1 ]; then say "  would append TOOLKIT_ENV_BLOCK"
else
  backup "$HOME/.bashrc"
  printf '\n%s\n[ -f "$HOME/.config/toolkit/env.sh" ] && . "$HOME/.config/toolkit/env.sh"\n# <<< TOOLKIT_ENV_BLOCK <<<\n' "$MARK" >> "$HOME/.bashrc"
  if bash -n "$HOME/.bashrc"; then say "  appended; bash -n ok"; else fail "bashrc syntax after edit (restore from $BK/.bashrc)"; fi
fi

say "== SHA-256 table"; { printf 'state\titem\tbefore\tafter\n'; printf '%s\n' "${TABLE[@]}"; } | { if [ "$DRY" = 1 ]; then cat; else tee "$HOME/.config/.toolkit-backups/apply-$TS.txt"; fi; }
say "== summary: changed=$CHANGED same=$SAME fails=$FAILS backup=$([ "$DRY" = 1 ] && echo none || echo "$BK")"
[ "$FAILS" -eq 0 ]
Shell cwd was reset to /home/anant_gupta

#!/usr/bin/env bash
# 38-toolkit-upgrade.sh (Build 5b, 2026-10-13). Conservative, reversible, user-space upgrades of the WSL toolkit. No sudo.
# Every download is pinned to an exact version and verified against a pinned SHA-256 (GitHub release asset digests, read 2026-10-13).
# The previous binary is moved to ~/.local/bin/.prev/<tool>.<timestamp> first, so any tool can be rolled back.
# Usage: 38-toolkit-upgrade.sh [--dry-run] [--only gh,fzf,rg,fd,bat,lazygit,sesh,ghdash,node,pnpm,tmux] [--rollback TOOL]
# Default set: gh fzf rg fd bat lazygit sesh ghdash node pnpm tmux. pnpm is pinned to 12.4.2 via corepack (both laptops, user decision 2026-10-14).
# tmux 3.8 builds from source (static libevent, yacc stub, no sudo) and is skipped while a tmux server is running, because a 3.8 client cannot attach to a 3.4 server.
# NOT upgraded on purpose: git, jq, direnv, ncdu (apt; no AI-dev benefit, risk of config drift), uv (0.13 is a new minor), starship,
# yazi, delta, zoxide, atuin (already the latest release), claude, codex (already the latest release).
set -uo pipefail
DRY=0; ONLY="gh,fzf,rg,fd,bat,lazygit,sesh,ghdash,node,pnpm,tmux"; ROLL=""
while [ $# -gt 0 ]; do
  case "$1" in
    --dry-run) DRY=1 ;; --only) shift; ONLY="${1:-}" ;; --rollback) shift; ROLL="${1:-}" ;;
    --help|-h) sed -n '2,12p' "$0"; exit 0 ;; *) echo "unknown option: $1" >&2; exit 2 ;;
  esac; shift
done
export PATH="$HOME/.local/bin:$HOME/.cargo/bin:/usr/local/bin:/usr/bin:/bin:$PATH"
BIN="$HOME/.local/bin"; PREV="$BIN/.prev"; TS=$(date +%Y%m%d-%H%M%S); FAILS=0; mkdir -p "$BIN" "$PREV"
say() { printf '%s\n' "$*"; }; fail() { say "FAIL: $*"; FAILS=$((FAILS+1)); }
want() { case ",$ONLY," in *",$1,"*) return 0;; *) return 1;; esac; }
W=$(mktemp -d); trap 'rm -rf "$W"' EXIT

if [ -n "$ROLL" ]; then
  last=$(ls -1t "$PREV/$ROLL".* 2>/dev/null | head -n 1)
  [ -n "$last" ] || { say "no backup for $ROLL in $PREV"; exit 1; }
  mv -f "$last" "$BIN/$ROLL" && say "rolled $ROLL back from $last"; exit 0
fi

fetch() { # url sha256 out
  curl -fsSL -m 300 "$1" -o "$3" || return 1
  [ "$(sha256sum "$3" | cut -d' ' -f1)" = "$2" ] || { say "  checksum mismatch for $1"; return 1; }
}
put_bin() { # src name
  local src="$1" name="$2" dst="$BIN/$2"
  if [ -e "$dst" ] || [ -L "$dst" ]; then mv -f "$dst" "$PREV/$name.$TS" || return 1; fi
  install -m 0755 "$src" "$dst.new" && mv -f "$dst.new" "$dst"
}
have() { # bin expected-substring
  local v; v=$("$1" --version 2>&1 | head -n 3 | tr '\n' ' '); case "$v" in *"$2"*) return 0;; *) return 1;; esac
}
tarball() { # name verstr bin url sha inner
  local name="$1" ver="$2" bin="$3" url="$4" sha="$5" inner="$6"
  want "$name" || return 0
  if have "$bin" "$ver" && [ "$(command -v "$bin")" = "$BIN/$bin" ]; then say "  $name already $ver in ~/.local/bin"; return 0; fi
  if [ "$DRY" = 1 ]; then say "  would install $name $ver from $url"; return 0; fi
  mkdir -p "$W/$name"
  if ! { fetch "$url" "$sha" "$W/$name.tgz" && tar -xzf "$W/$name.tgz" -C "$W/$name"; }; then fail "$name download/extract"; return 1; fi
  put_bin "$W/$name/$inner" "$bin" || { fail "$name install"; return 1; }
  if have "$bin" "$ver"; then say "  $name -> $("$bin" --version 2>&1 | head -n 1)"; else fail "$name version check after install (rollback: $0 --rollback $bin)"; fi
}

say "== Build 5b toolkit upgrade ($TS) dry-run=$DRY only=$ONLY"
tarball gh      "2.102.0" gh      https://github.com/cli/cli/releases/download/v2.102.0/gh_2.102.0_linux_amd64.tar.gz                     [REDACTED] "gh_2.102.0_linux_amd64/bin/gh"
tarball fzf     "0.74.5"  fzf     https://github.com/junegunn/fzf/releases/download/v0.74.5/fzf-0.74.5-linux_amd64.tar.gz                  [REDACTED] "fzf"
tarball rg      "15.2.0"  rg      https://github.com/BurntSushi/ripgrep/releases/download/15.2.0/ripgrep-15.2.[REDACTED].tar.gz [REDACTED] "ripgrep-15.2.[REDACTED]/rg"
tarball fd      "10.5.0"  fd      https://github.com/sharkdp/fd/releases/download/v10.5.0/fd-v10.5.[REDACTED].tar.gz     [REDACTED] "fd-v10.5.[REDACTED]/fd"
tarball bat     "0.26.1"  bat     https://github.com/sharkdp/bat/releases/download/v0.26.1/bat-v0.26.[REDACTED].tar.gz   [REDACTED] "bat-v0.26.[REDACTED]/bat"
tarball lazygit "0.66.1"  lazygit  https://github.com/jesseduffield/lazygit/releases/download/v0.66.1/lazygit_0.66.1_linux_x86_64.tar.gz  [REDACTED] "lazygit"
tarball sesh    "2.33.0"  sesh    https://github.com/joshmedeski/sesh/releases/download/v2.33.0/sesh_Linux_x86_64.tar.gz                  [REDACTED] "sesh"

if want ghdash; then
  if [ "$DRY" = 1 ]; then say "  would run: gh extension upgrade dlvhdr/gh-dash"
  else gh extension upgrade dlvhdr/gh-dash 2>&1 | tail -n 1; say "  gh-dash: $(gh extension list 2>/dev/null | grep -i dash | cut -f1,3 | tr '\t' ' ')"; fi
fi

if want node; then
  export NVM_DIR="$HOME/.nvm"
  # shellcheck disable=SC1091
  [ -s "$NVM_DIR/nvm.sh" ] && . "$NVM_DIR/nvm.sh"
  CUR=$(node -v 2>/dev/null)
  if [ "$CUR" = "v24.21.0" ]; then say "  node already v24.21.0"
  elif [ "$DRY" = 1 ]; then say "  would run: nvm install 24.21.0 --reinstall-packages-from=$CUR ; corepack enable ; keep $CUR installed"
  else
    nvm install 24.21.0 --reinstall-packages-from="$CUR" >"$W/nvm.log" 2>&1 || { tail -n 5 "$W/nvm.log"; fail "nvm install 24.21.0"; }
    nvm alias default 'lts/*' >/dev/null 2>&1; nvm use 24.21.0 >/dev/null 2>&1
    corepack enable >/dev/null 2>&1
    say "  node $(node -v) npm $(npm -v) pnpm $(pnpm --version 2>&1 | tail -n 1) ; globals: $(npm ls -g --depth=0 2>/dev/null | tail -n +2 | wc -l) ; old $CUR kept"
  fi
fi

if want pnpm; then
  export NVM_DIR="$HOME/.nvm"
  # shellcheck disable=SC1091
  [ -s "$NVM_DIR/nvm.sh" ] && . "$NVM_DIR/nvm.sh"
  if [ "$(pnpm --version 2>/dev/null | tail -n 1)" = "12.4.2" ]; then say "  pnpm already 12.4.2"
  elif [ "$DRY" = 1 ]; then say "  would run: corepack enable ; corepack prepare pnpm@12.4.2 --activate"
  else
    corepack enable >/dev/null 2>&1
    corepack prepare pnpm@12.4.2 --activate >/dev/null 2>&1 || fail "corepack prepare pnpm@12.4.2"
    say "  pnpm $(pnpm --version 2>&1 | tail -n 1)"
  fi
fi

if want tmux; then
  LE="$HOME/.local/libevent-2.1.13"
  if tmux ls >/dev/null 2>&1; then say "  tmux: a server is running; refusing to build (kill it first: tmux kill-server)"
  elif [ "$(tmux -V 2>&1)" = "tmux 3.8" ] && [ "$(command -v tmux)" = "$BIN/tmux" ]; then say "  tmux already 3.8"
  elif [ "$DRY" = 1 ]; then say "  would build libevent 2.1.13 (static, ~/.local/libevent) and tmux 3.8 into ~/.local/bin"
  elif ! { command -v gcc >/dev/null && command -v make >/dev/null && command -v pkg-config >/dev/null; }; then fail "tmux build needs gcc, make, pkg-config"
  else
    # configure insists on yacc, but the release tarball ships a pre-generated cmd-parse.c, so a no-op stub is enough (no sudo needed)
    if ! { command -v yacc >/dev/null || command -v bison >/dev/null; }; then
      mkdir -p "$W/stub"; printf '#!/bin/sh\nexit 0\n' > "$W/stub/yacc"; chmod +x "$W/stub/yacc"; export PATH="$W/stub:$PATH"
      say "  no yacc/bison: using a stub (the tarball ships cmd-parse.c)"
    fi
    if ! ( cd "$W" && fetch https://github.com/libevent/libevent/releases/download/release-2.1.13-stable/libevent-2.1.13-stable.tar.gz [REDACTED] le.tgz \
      && tar -xzf le.tgz && cd libevent-2.1.13-stable && ./configure --prefix="$LE" --disable-shared --disable-openssl --disable-samples --disable-libevent-regress >/dev/null && make -j4 >/dev/null && make install >/dev/null ); then fail "libevent build"; fi
    if ! ( cd "$W" && fetch https://github.com/tmux/tmux/releases/download/3.8/tmux-3.8.tar.gz [REDACTED] tmux.tgz \
      && tar -xzf tmux.tgz && cd tmux-3.8 && PKG_CONFIG_PATH="$LE/lib/pkgconfig" ./configure --prefix="$HOME/.local" >/dev/null && make -j4 >/dev/null ); then fail "tmux build"; fi
    if [ "$FAILS" -eq 0 ]; then
      if put_bin "$W/tmux-3.8/tmux" tmux; then say "  tmux -> $(tmux -V)"; else fail "tmux install"; fi
    fi
  fi
fi

say "== versions now"
for t in gh fzf rg fd bat lazygit sesh tmux node pnpm; do printf '  %-8s %s\n' "$t" "$({ if [ "$t" = tmux ]; then tmux -V; else "$t" --version; fi; } 2>&1 | head -n 1 | cut -c1-70) [$(command -v "$t")]"; done
say "== done: fails=$FAILS ; previous binaries are in $PREV ; rollback: $0 --rollback <tool>"
[ "$FAILS" -eq 0 ]

#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Weekly cache cleanup - clears regenerable package-manager caches only.
# Never touches ~/.cargo, ~/.rustup, ~/.nvm (toolchains, not caches) or project data.
set -uo pipefail

log_dir="$HOME/tools/logs"
mkdir -p "$log_dir"
log_file="$log_dir/cleanup-$(date +%Y-%m-%d_%H%M).log"

exec >>"$log_file" 2>&1
echo "=== Weekly cache cleanup started $(date) ==="

if command -v uv >/dev/null 2>&1; then
  echo "-- uv cache clean --"
  uv cache clean
fi

if command -v npm >/dev/null 2>&1; then
  echo "-- npm cache clean --force --"
  npm cache clean --force
fi

if [ -d "$HOME/.cache/node" ]; then
  before=$(du -sh "$HOME/.cache/node" 2>/dev/null | cut -f1)
  rm -rf "${HOME:?}/.cache/node"/*
  echo "Cleared ~/.cache/node (was $before)"
fi

echo "=== Weekly cache cleanup finished $(date) ==="

# Keep only the last 12 logs (~3 months of weekly runs)
find "$log_dir" -name 'cleanup-*.log' -type f -printf '%T@ %p\n' 2>/dev/null \
  | sort -rn | tail -n +13 | cut -d' ' -f2- | xargs -r rm -f
  ```

== env.sh
# ~/.config/toolkit/env.sh - golden, Build 5b. Sourced from ~/.bashrc by ONE guarded line (TOOLKIT_ENV_BLOCK).
# Holds no secrets. Safe to source twice. Interactive shells only.
case $- in *i*) ;; *) return 0 2>/dev/null ;; esac

export RIPGREP_CONFIG_PATH="$HOME/.config/ripgrep/config"

if command -v fd >/dev/null 2>&1; then
  export FZF_DEFAULT_COMMAND='fd --type f --hidden --follow --exclude .git'
  export FZF_CTRL_T_COMMAND="$FZF_DEFAULT_COMMAND"
  export FZF_ALT_C_COMMAND='fd --type d --hidden --follow --exclude .git'
fi
# Tokyo Night Storm colours for fzf
export FZF_DEFAULT_OPTS='--height=40% --layout=reverse --border --info=inline --color=fg:#c0caf5,bg:#24283b,hl:#7aa2f7,fg+:#c0caf5,bg+:#292e42,hl+:#7dcfff,info:#7aa2f7,prompt:#7dcfff,pointer:#bb9af7,marker:#9ece6a,spinner:#bb9af7,header:#565f89,border:#565f89'

# fzf shell integration (fzf >= 0.48): Ctrl-T files, Alt-C directories. Ctrl-R is left to atuin (FZF_CTRL_R_COMMAND= disables fzf's binding).
if command -v fzf >/dev/null 2>&1 && fzf --bash >/dev/null 2>&1; then
  FZF_CTRL_R_COMMAND= eval "$(fzf --bash)"
fi

import os,re

start=s.index('# JARVIS_WSL_ENV_BLOCK'); end=s.index('eval "$(direnv hook bash)"')+len('eval "$(direnv hook bash)"')
new='''# JARVIS_WSL_ENV_BLOCK (hardened 2026-10-15: guarded hooks, no duplicate PATH or nvm)

if [ -f "$HOME/.atuin/bin/env" ] && command -v atuin >/dev/null 2>&1; then
  . "$HOME/.atuin/bin/env"
  eval "$(atuin init bash)"
fi

command -v direnv >/dev/null 2>&1 && eval "$(direnv hook bash)"'''
open(p,'w').write(s[:start]+new+s[end:])
EOF

- **Notes:** I patched 16 notes in Jarvis: the Build 5b handoff, the Build 2 Acer Handoff, SSH Runbook and findings, the Index, Runs Index, Installations, Ubuntu - WSL, Acer Live State, the Snapshot notes, Config Reference, scripts README, Acer Run Sheet, the Build 5 findings status and the session log. Backups of each are in `Runs/_backups/acer-visit-20261015/`.
- **Check script:** `36-wsl-check.sh` in the vault now works on both laptops.

## Problems I found
1. **`31-wsl-sudo-step.sh` would fail on the Acer.** It also runs `systemctl disable --now ollama`, and the Acer has no WSL Ollama service, so with `set -e` it would stop before its checks. That is why `socat` gets its own command below.
2. **The Acer's internship clone holds 4 commits that exist nowhere else.** Its GitHub branch was deleted. I made a verified Git bundle at `D:\Backup\repo-bundles\[REDACTED].bundle` and pushed nothing. The Dell's internship checkout is 6 ahead and 5 behind with 25 dirty files. The Acer's commits adopt uv, while the Dell note says that repo is not a uv project, so the two need a deliberate merge.
3. **The Windows toolkit scripts fail from a shell that inherited PowerShell 7's `PSModulePath`.** They stop with "Get-FileHash not recognized" before doing anything. Resetting `PSModulePath` fixed it. This is noted in the Run Sheet.
4. **The Acer-to-Dell link is slow.** A 512 MiB SSH stream ran at 2.5 MiB/s on a direct path. I changed nothing. Don't clone the Dell's 1.5 GB `jan` repo over it until that is understood.

## Commands for you
**1. `socat` (in WSL)**
```bash
sudo apt-get update && sudo apt-get install -y socat bubblewrap && socat -V | sed -n 2p
```

**2. Windows toolkit Part 1 (a normal Windows PowerShell window, editors closed, accept the UAC prompts)**
```powershell
$V  = 'D:\_Anant\20_Progress\Documents\Jarvis'
$TK = "$V\40_Resources\CS\Concepts\New Laptop\Old Laptop Rebuild\Windows Toolkit"
powershell.exe -NoProfile -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File "$TK\_scripts\10-install-toolkit.ps1" -Machine acer
```
Then open a fresh terminal and run Part 3:
```powershell
powershell.exe -NoProfile -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File "$TK\_scripts\99-verify.ps1" -Machine acer
powershell.exe -NoProfile -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File "$TK\_scripts\40-build-catalog.ps1" -Machine acer
```
Tell me when it's done and I'll record the results in the notes.

**3. Host-key read on the Dell (WSL, needs sudo)**

The accepted fingerprint is `SHA256:[REDACTED]`. I haven't confirmed the key file's location, so list it first:
```bash
sudo find /var/lib/tailscale -iname '*ssh_host*'
sudo ssh-keygen -lf <path printed above, the .pub file if present>
```

## Decisions for you
- The Acer's 4 internship commits: push the branch, or bring them to the Dell session?
- Promote the Acer's real bat and fd configs into the shared `Windows Toolkit/configs/`? The Dell would then need to re-run `20-apply-configs.ps1`.
- Narrow how `~/.mcp.env` is sourced?
- Update the Acer's WSL (2.7.14.0, the Dell is on 3.0.3.0)?
- Approve the per-repo `packageManager` pins (none of your repos set one).
- The Acer's Linux disk is 39 GiB and 72% used. It needs a backup before any resize.

**Tool calls:**

- `Bash` — `cd "/mnt/d/_Anant/20_Progress/Documents/Jarvis/40_Resources/CS/Concepts/New Laptop/Old Laptop Rebuild"; cat "Old Laptop Rebuild - Build 2 Acer Handoff.md"; echo =========; cat "Windows Toolkit/Acer Run Sheet - Windows Toolkit.md"; echo ========; cat "WSL Parity/_scripts/31-wsl-sudo-step.sh"; ls /mnt/d/Backup /mnt/d/Backup/Quarantine 2>&1 | head -20`
  ```

# Old Laptop Rebuild - Build 2 Acer Handoff

## Ready-to-run prompt for the Acer Codex session

You are configuring the Acer as a client of the Dell's canonical WSL checkout. Follow the Locked Decisions note. Do not copy or sync code, node_modules, `.venv`, logs, caches, toolchains, or agent state. Do not touch Acer secrets or Jarvis/The Plan vault sync. Do not change Windows VS Code user settings or Settings Sync without approval. Remote-SSH user settings are synced and changes made there can reach the Dell, so inspect and avoid edits to synced user settings unless explicitly approved.

### 1. Confirm Tailscale placement
Tailscale belongs on the Acer Windows side only. The user reports that the official Windows client is already installed, authenticated to the same admin-console account as Dell, and registered as Windows node `gupta-builds` (`100.84.91.11`). Do not reinstall or sign out. Before connecting, read-only verify Tailscale is not installed/active inside any Acer WSL distro. If it is installed in WSL, stop and report exact distro/package/service evidence; do not remove it without approval. Do not install or run Tailscale in Acer WSL.

The user reports Dell `dell-wsl` is online at `100.94.129.113`, Tailscale 1.104.1 stable, SSH advertised, and key expiry disabled. Full MagicDNS name is `dell-wsl.tail0c1d06.ts.net`. The user inspected Access Controls: the existing SSH rule is `check` from `autogroup:member` to `autogroup:self` as `autogroup:nonroot`/`root`; no policy edit was made.

### 2. Configure plain Windows OpenSSH and VS Code Remote-SSH
Use the Windows OpenSSH executable and no wrapper, ProxyCommand, or WSL Tailscale. Add this host entry to the user's SSH config only with approval:

```sshconfig
Host dell-wsl
    HostName dell-wsl
    User anant_gupta
    ServerAliveInterval 30
    ServerAliveCountMax 3
```

If MagicDNS name resolution fails, obtain the Dell's current tailnet IPv4 with `tailscale ip -4` on the Dell and set `HostName` to that address. Prefer the `dell-wsl` MagicDNS name when it resolves. In VS Code, install/use Remote - SSH, select `dell-wsl`, and open only the canonical paths under `/home/anant_gupta/projects` on the remote host. Do not add remote settings to Acer's synced user settings without approval.

Observed: Acer Windows `ssh anant_gupta@dell-wsl` initially showed a first-connect prompt for `dell-wsl (100.94.129.113)`, ED25519 fingerprint `SHA256:[REDACTED]`. The user entered the displayed fingerprint and it was added to `known_hosts`; this proves acceptance, not an independent comparison with Tailscale's advertised host key. Do not remove or rewrite `known_hosts`. Run the read-only advertised-key comparison below and stop if the fingerprint differs or is unavailable.

The user's first connection showed an ED25519 fingerprint and they accepted the displayed fingerprint. That is not an independent verification. Complete the read-only advertised-key comparison in Step 3; do not edit `known_hosts`.

### 3. Verify the route and remote execution
Current Acer evidence: `tailscale.exe ping dell-wsl` returned several DERP replies, then a direct `via 10.5.8.253:41641 in 7ms`. Plain `ssh.exe` completed and ran hostname, path, and Git status commands on Dell, showing `anant_gupta`, `/home/anant_gupta`, and the canonical checkout. Earlier Remote-SSH terminal output also showed the repository root and Dell WSL kernel. The single modified `.codex/hooks/session-wrapup.ps1` is pre-existing user work; do not reset, stash, stage, commit, or otherwise alter it.

Run the remaining identity comparison in Acer PowerShell:

```powershell
$status = tailscale.exe status --json | ConvertFrom-Json
$peer = $status.Peer.PSObject.Properties.Value | Where-Object {
    $_.DNSName -like 'dell-wsl*' -or $_.TailscaleIPs -contains '100.94.129.113'
}
$keyFile = Join-Path $env:TEMP 'dell-wsl-tailnet-hostkeys.pub'
$peer.SSH_HostKeys | Set-Content -LiteralPath $keyFile -Encoding ascii
ssh-keygen.exe -lf $keyFile
```

Compare the printed ED25519 fingerprint exactly to `SHA256:[REDACTED]`. If the key is absent or differs, stop and report; do not rewrite `known_hosts` or accept another key. The temporary file contains a public host key only. The plain SSH and Remote-SSH tests have already completed, but independent key verification remains outstanding. Tailscale's `via IP:port` output indicates a direct path; earlier `via DERP(...)` replies show route establishment can initially relay before switching direct.

Run a large but read-only Git transfer check by fetching a substantial repository's refs/objects from GitHub on the Dell and measuring before/after bytes, or use an approved read-only large-object transfer. Do not clone a second working copy for this test. Report transfer size and duration. The live Tailscale path check must show `tailscale ping` direct; if it is relay-only, report that rather than assuming success.

Optional Acer WSL route test: without installing Tailscale inside Acer WSL, run `ssh anant_gupta@dell-wsl 'hostname'` and `tailscale ping dell-wsl` from an Acer WSL terminal. Whether Windows-side Tailscale provides that route to Acer WSL is not established by the docs and is **to be tested on the Acer**. If it fails, do not install Tailscale in Acer WSL and do not create wrappers; report the exact failure and ask the user before revisiting the locked design.

### 3b. Where to read the Dell's advertised host key, and the sync-check run (added 2026-10-15)
**Host key source.** The Dell cannot supply the key for this comparison: its own `tailscale status --json` has a `Self` entry with no `SSH_HostKeys` (checked 2026-10-15, Tailscale 1.104.1). The only independent source is the **Acer's** Tailscale view of the Dell: `tailscale.exe status --json`, then the `Peer` entry whose `DNSName` starts with `dell-wsl` (full name `dell-wsl.tail0c1d06.ts.net`, `100.94.129.113`), field `SSH_HostKeys`. That is exactly what the Step 3 snippet reads. If the Acer's peer entry also has no `SSH_HostKeys`, stop and report that; the fallback is a Dell-side read that needs `sudo`, which the user runs. Never rewrite `known_hosts`.

**sync-check over SSH (read-only).** Run from the Acer, then compare with the Dell's own run below:
```powershell
ssh dell-wsl '~/tools/sync-check'
```
It prints `host=`, tool versions, then for each of the two canonical repos: `branch`, `fetch=ok`, `compare_ref ... ahead/behind`, `dirty_count`. `git fetch` only updates remote-tracking refs; nothing in the working tree changes. **Ignore the `node`, `pnpm` and `uv` lines over plain SSH:** they print `not-installed` because nvm and `~/.local/bin` are set up in the interactive `.bashrc`, which a non-interactive SSH command does not load. The repo lines are the real check.

Dell's own run on 2026-10-15 (local, same script):
| Repo | Branch | Versus | Ahead / behind | Dirty |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `second-brain-claudekit` | `main` | `origin/main` | 0 / 0 | 1 |
| `internship-research-loop` | `dell-latitude/deadline-quota-and-reseed-fixes` | `origin/master` | 6 / 5 | 25 |
The second repo has diverged from `origin/master` and has 25 dirty files. Do not reset, stash, commit or push either repo from the Acer. Report what the Acer sees; the Dell session decides.

### 4. Inventory the Acer's existing clones
For both `second-brain-claudekit` and `internship-research-loop`, locate every clone and worktree on the Acer, then record path, branch/upstream, ahead/behind, dirty count, untracked files, and dependency/output sizes. Do not delete or overwrite anything. Identify whether each is canonical, a local-only backup, or a worktree. Present a per-path retirement/relabel proposal; retire only after the user approves and verifies needed commits/uncommitted files are protected. GitHub protects pushed commits only, not uncommitted work, ignored dependencies, or logs. Do not touch either vault's sync mechanism.

### 5. Report
Return evidence for: Tailscale Windows connected; no Tailscale inside Acer WSL; `tailscale ping`; plain Windows `ssh.exe`; the independent host-key comparison; Remote-SSH hostname/path; direct-path status; large-transfer bytes/time if a specific harmless test is approved; optional Acer WSL route result; Codex execution location; and full clone/worktree inventory with a safe retirement/relabel proposal. The identity comparison, clone inventory, large-transfer check, and optional Acer WSL route test remain pending. Mention that Remote-SSH user settings are synced and can flow back to Dell. Do not push, branch, commit, delete, or modify repo files without explicit approval.

# Acer Run Sheet - Windows Toolkit

## One-Line Answer
==Run Part 0 on the Acer first (it changes nothing), paste the tables back, and only then run the install and config steps; the Dell has already been through all of them and its versions are recorded in `toolkit.lock.json`.==

## Who this is for
The Claude session or the user on the Acer, Windows side, in a **normal** (not elevated) PowerShell. The Dell session has no access to the Acer. This sheet covers the Windows toolkit only. The WSL toolkit is a separate visit note: [[Acer Session Handoff - Toolkit Sync (Build 5b)]]. Do both in one Acer visit.

## What is different on the Acer (read this first)
| Item | Dell | Acer | Why it matters |
|---|---|---|---|
| Vault root | `D:\Users\_Anant\10_Areas\Documents\Jarvis` | `D:\_Anant\20_Progress\Documents\Jarvis` | The scripts find the vault by environment variable, then by candidate paths that contain `AGENTS.md`. The role (`acer`) is detected from this path. Pass `-Machine acer` anyway. |
| Computer name | `DESKTOP-3VBG0JH` | blank in the manifest | Role detection must not rely on the name. |
| Tailscale | absent on Windows (runs inside the Dell's WSL as `dell-wsl`) | installed on Windows as `gupta-builds` | Verify only. Never install it in the Acer's WSL. Never reinstall or sign out on Windows. |
| GPU | Intel Iris Xe only | RTX 5070 Ti | Heavy local models belong here, not on the Dell. |
| Kiro, Miniconda, opam | present (Dell-only in the manifest) | not part of this toolkit | Do not install them. |
| Chocolatey and Python 3.13.5 | retired on the Dell | not part of this toolkit | Do not run `04-admin-remove.ps1` or `11-node-lts-swap.ps1` on the Acer. Both are Dell-only. |
| Font | per-user install on C: (about 760 MB, two sets) | system-wide | The install script adopts what exists. |
| VS Code | on C: | on D:\Apps | The scripts only verify it. They never write VS Code, MCP or Claude Code settings. Settings Sync is live. |
| Terminal Ubuntu profile | `Ubuntu (Dell)` | `Ubuntu-24.04 (Acer)` | Machine profiles are preserved by the Terminal merge. |
| Node | LTS 24 (was 22) | check; the sheet does not change it | `10-install-toolkit.ps1` installs LTS if missing. Any Node major change is a decision for the user. |

## Where the scripts are on the Acer
Syncthing carries the vault. Wait until it finishes, then:
```powershell
$V  = 'D:\_Anant\20_Progress\Documents\Jarvis'
$TK = "$V\40_Resources\CS\Concepts\New Laptop\Old Laptop Rebuild\Windows Toolkit"
Test-Path "$TK\_scripts\00-preflight.ps1"      # must print True
Test-Path "$V\AGENTS.md"                        # must print True
```
If the vault is elsewhere: `ls D:\_Anant\*\Documents` and set `$V` to the folder that contains `AGENTS.md`, or set the user variable `TOOLKIT_VAULT_ROOT` to it.

Command template for every script (quote the full path, run as a file):
```powershell
powershell.exe -NoProfile -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File "$TK\_scripts\<script>.ps1" -Machine acer [switches]
```
Every script prints a result table (`ID STATUS DETAIL`), writes a log to `D:\WSL\ops\windows\logs\` and a result JSON to `$TK\results\<host>\`. A script that fails a required check exits non-zero. Statuses: `PASS`, `INFO`, `PLAN` (dry run), `PENDING`, `DIFF` (differs from the source, nothing was overwritten), `WARN`, `FAIL`.

## Part 0 - read-only paste-back (run this first, nothing changes)
The Acer already has parts of the toolkit from 2026-10-12 (fzf, fd, bat, delta, zoxide, PSFzf), so start by finding out exactly what is present.
```powershell
powershell.exe -NoProfile -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File "$TK\_scripts\00-preflight.ps1" -Machine acer
powershell.exe -NoProfile -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File "$TK\_scripts\10-install-toolkit.ps1" -Machine acer -DryRun
powershell.exe -NoProfile -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File "$TK\_scripts\20-apply-configs.ps1" -Machine acer -DryRun
powershell.exe -NoProfile -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File "$TK\_scripts\99-verify.ps1" -Machine acer
```
Do not pass `-WriteLock` on the Acer: only the Dell writes `toolkit.lock.json`. `00` prints the machine, vault, manifest, a secret scan of the shared configs and the plan. `10 -DryRun` lists what it would install or adopt. `20 -DryRun` lists each config as MATCH, DIFF or MISSING without writing. `99-verify` compares the manifest with reality; Dell-only rows show as skipped.

**Paste back** the four result tables (the `ID STATUS DETAIL` blocks) plus the four result JSON paths, and this one-liner:
```powershell
winget list --disable-interactivity | Select-String -Pattern 'Tailscale|PowerShell|Starship|NanaZip|Docker|Ollama|Cursor|Antigravity|Visual Studio Code|Claude'
```
The Dell session reads both result sets and writes the parity matrix (PASS or DIFF with a reason). Do Part 1 only after that, or if the user says go.

## Part 1 - install and adopt (normal shell, UAC prompts)
Close editors first (the Git upgrade replaces files in use). UAC prompts appear for Git, GitHub CLI, Starship and Node; accept them.
```powershell
powershell.exe -NoProfile -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File "$TK\_scripts\10-install-toolkit.ps1" -Machine acer
```
Prints one line per tool (`installed=... version=...`) and a summary. It continues past a single failed package. It never installs Tailscale, never removes anything and never touches Node's major version. Needs: internet, `winget`.

## Part 2 - apply the shared configs
```powershell
powershell.exe -NoProfile -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File "$TK\_scripts\20-apply-configs.ps1" -Machine acer
```
Writes (after a timestamped backup to `D:\Backup\Toolkit\<timestamp>\`): the managed profile block in both PowerShell profiles, `starship.toml`, the global git ignore, the shared delta include, bat and fd configs, the Terminal fragment merged into `settings.json` (Acer profiles preserved), git settings and the toolkit environment variables, and the user PATH order (standalone uv first). An existing bat or fd file is kept and reported as `DIFF`: the Acer's real files are the source, so a DIFF there means the shared copy in the vault needs updating, not the Acer. Tell the Dell session. Roll back with `-RestoreFrom "D:\Backup\Toolkit\<timestamp>"`.

## Part 3 - verify and catalog
Open a **fresh** terminal window (PATH and profile must reload), then:
```powershell
powershell.exe -NoProfile -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File "$TK\_scripts\99-verify.ps1" -Machine acer
powershell.exe -NoProfile -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File "$TK\_scripts\40-build-catalog.ps1" -Machine acer
```
The second writes `$TK\catalog\Tool Catalog - <host>.md` and `.json`. It never prints secrets or `.bashrc`. Paste back the `99-verify` table and the catalog's file name; Syncthing carries the catalog to the Dell.

## Do not run on the Acer
`11-node-lts-swap.ps1`, `04-admin-remove.ps1`, `60-build5b-host.ps1`, `05-startup-cleanup.ps1`, `06-ollama-check.ps1` and anything in `D:\WSL\ops` that belongs to the Dell. Never `wsl --shutdown` from inside WSL.

## After it ran
Say so in the Dell session. It reads both result sets (`results\DESKTOP-3VBG0JH` and the Acer's folder) and writes the parity matrix into [[Old Laptop Rebuild - Build 4 Windows Toolkit Findings]]. Known differences to expect: Terminal version (the Dell stays on 1.24), VS Code location, font scope, Kiro and the Dell-only runtimes, and pnpm: the Windows winget pnpm is 12.11.2 on the Dell while the WSL pnpm on both laptops is Corepack-pinned to 12.4.2.

#!/usr/bin/env bash
# 31-wsl-sudo-step.sh (Build 5). Run once:  sudo bash 31-wsl-sudo-step.sh
# Step 1 apt update, 2 install socat (bubblewrap is already present; named so apt keeps it),
# 3 stop+disable the WSL Ollama service (binary kept) so the Windows Ollama can own port 11434,
# 4 verify. Does NOT touch AppArmor or any sandbox setting: the agent sandbox stays off.
set -euo pipefail
[ "$(id -u)" -eq 0 ] || { echo "Run with sudo: sudo bash $0"; exit 1; }
step() { printf '\n== STEP %s: %s\n' "$1" "$2"; }

step 1 "apt-get update"
apt-get update

step 2 "install socat (and keep bubblewrap)"
DEBIAN_FRONTEND=noninteractive apt-get install -y socat bubblewrap

step 3 "stop and disable the WSL ollama service (binary stays)"
systemctl disable --now ollama

step 4 "verify"
printf 'socat   : '; socat -V | sed -n 2p
printf 'bwrap   : '; bwrap --version
printf 'ollama enabled: '; systemctl is-enabled ollama || true
printf 'ollama active : '; systemctl is-active ollama || true
printf 'binary kept   : '; ls -l /usr/local/bin/ollama
if ss -ltn | grep -q ':11434 '; then echo "WARNING: something still listens on 11434"; ss -ltnp | grep ':11434 '; exit 1; else echo "port 11434 is free"; fi
echo "Sandbox settings untouched. Done."
ls: cannot access '/mnt/d/Backup': No such file or directory
ls: cannot access '/mnt/d/Backup/Quarantine': No such file or directory
Shell cwd was reset to /home/anant_gupta

# Jarvis Agent Guide

This vault is an AI-assisted second brain built in Obsidian. Treat it as a knowledge system first and a file tree second.

## Priority Files

- Read [[Jarvis OS — North Star]] for *why Jarvis exists, why it underperforms, and the target state*. It is the strategy spine; this file owns only the write contract and routing pointers. Do not duplicate its philosophy here.
- Read [[60_Claude/07_AI_Information/Vault Map]] first — the five-minute orientation for any agent.
- Read [[40_Resources/Obsidian/Jarvis Vault Architecture]] for where every note goes (the placement source of truth).
- Read `30_Order/` before writing any note — its `Workflows/` and `Templates/` are the structural half of [[HUMAN_WRITING]].
- Read [[AI_CONTEXT]] for the shared cross-tool context manifest.
- Read [[00_Dashboard]] for the current control panel.
- Read [[CLAUDE]] for the existing Claude-layer workflow.
- Read [[40_Resources/Obsidian/Vault Operating System]] for the property schema before creating or restructuring notes.
- Read [[HUMAN_WRITING]] before drafting or rewriting prose.

## Folder Roles
Full folder definitions: [[40_Resources/Obsidian/Jarvis Vault Architecture]].

## Write Contract

Every agent follows this, regardless of which tool is driving. Full version: [[40_Resources/Obsidian/Jarvis Vault Architecture]].

### Golden rules

1. **Never create a new top-level file or folder at the vault root.** Root holds only `00_Dashboard.md`, `AGENTS.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, `HUMAN_WRITING.md`, and the numbered folders `10_Areas`–`60_Claude`. This is the single most damaging mistake an agent can make.
2. **When unsure where a note goes, write it to `60_Claude/00_Inbox/`.** Unsure is the trigger to use the Inbox, never to invent a location.
3. **Read `30_Order/` before writing** — its `Templates/` and `Workflows/` are the structural half of [[HUMAN_WRITING]].
4. **Search before creating.** Extend an existing canonical note instead of duplicating.
5. **Preserve frontmatter and wikilinks. Patch by heading.**

6. **No personal-life content in Jarvis.** Jarvis is the execution/technical workshop — course work, projects, career mechanics, business/project income. Health, personal finance, relationships, reflective/confessional journaling, and identity-as-a-person content live in The Plan (`00_Live/`). `10_Areas/Life/Truths of Life/` is the one exception, and it's scoped narrowly to builder-identity evidence (what the work says about me as an engineer), never personal reflection — see the scope rule in that folder's notes.

### Where does this note go?

| If the note is… | Write it to… | Standards doc to read first |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Raw clip, paste, web capture, video, imported source | `60_Claude/05_Clippings/` |  |
| Quick AI output you're unsure how to file | `60_Claude/00_Inbox/` |  |
| Summary of one source | `60_Claude/10_Source_Summaries/` | [[Source Summary Standard]] |
| Reusable distilled knowledge (a concept, not a source) | `60_Claude/20_Distilled_Notes/` → promote to `40_Resources/` or `10_Areas/` once stable | [[Evergreen Standard]] |
| Stable reference material (guide, cheat sheet, plugin doc, link) | `40_Resources/` + backlink to its `10_Areas/` domain |  |
| Active project, internship, research, mentorship work | `20_Progress/` under the matching project | [[Project Standard]] |
| Canonical fact about a life domain | `10_Areas/` — patch by heading; no new top-level files without instruction |  |
| Synthesized project brief | `60_Claude/40_Project_Briefs/` |  |
| Reusable output artifact (story, bullet, prompt) | `60_Claude/35_Outputs/` with `source_concepts:` provenance |  |
| Daily / weekly / monthly review | `60_Claude/50_Reviews/` |  |
| Dashboard or index | `60_Claude/44_Indexes/` |  |
| Session log entry | append to `60_Claude/07_AI_Information/Session Logs/log.md` |  |
| New template, writing workflow, or CLI tool | `30_Order/` (only when explicitly building one) |  |
| Visualization for a concept / project / source | `10_Areas/Excalidraw/` |  |
| Information about the whole vault for any AI tool | `60_Claude/07_AI_Information/` |  |

### Never write to

- The vault root — no new files or folders directly inside `D:\Users\_Anant\10_Areas\Documents\Jarvis\`.
- `50_Archive/` — never read, never write.
- `60_Claude/05_Clippings/` after capture — raw sources are read-only.
- `40_Resources/` in bulk — curated hub; add one backlinked entry at a time, never a batch of AI distillations.
- `.obsidian/`, `.claude/`, `.cursor/`, `.kiro/`, `.codex`, `.git/` — settings and tooling only, never notes.

## Working Rules
- **Codex model routing:** use the global `base` profile for bounded, routine work with explicit inputs and acceptance criteria. Use `deep` before consequential architecture, hard debugging, security or code review, cross-source synthesis, important decisions, or difficult coursework. If base work becomes cross-cutting or difficult to verify, stop and ask to continue in `deep`; do not silently change model behavior.
- **Codex output:** never use an em dash. Use complete sentences with proper grammar; do not omit words that carry meaning.
- Search before creating a note. Prefer extending an existing canonical note over making duplicates.
- Preserve frontmatter and use the vault schema fields consistently. Update `updated:` when a note changes meaningfully.
- Prefer Obsidian wikilinks for internal references.
- Use `next:` on project, plan, and active progress notes when a concrete next step exists.
- For continuity, read `AI_CONTEXT.md`, `00_Dashboard.md`, and `Session Logs/log.md` before making assumptions about current state.
- Do not write AI slop. Follow [[HUMAN_WRITING]]: cut filler, prefer mechanism and contrast, use real examples from this vault, leave notes denser and more human than before.
- After meaningful vault changes, append a concise continuity entry to `60_Claude/07_AI_Information/Session Logs/log.md`.
- Formatting rules (blank lines, markers, frontmatter, quality gate, safety): [[Jarvis Writing and Formatting]].

## Retrieval Rules

- Prefer structured fields over ad hoc prose when the information should be queryable.
- Prefer backlinks and MOC/index notes over duplicate summaries.
- Use dashboards and indexes under `60_Claude/44_Indexes/` to find gaps before inventing new structure.

## Safety

- This vault contains local plugin configuration. Do not surface or copy secrets from plugin data files.
- Prefer env vars and ignored local config for credentials, never vault notes.

  ```
import os, re, shutil, sys

ROOT = "/mnt/d/_Anant/20_Progress/Documents/Jarvis"
NL = f"{ROOT}/40_Resources/CS/Concepts/New Laptop"
OLR = f"{NL}/Old Laptop Rebuild"
BK = f"{OLR}/Runs/_backups/acer-visit-20261015"
os.makedirs(BK, exist_ok=True)
DATE = "2026-10-15"

def rd(p):
    with open(p, newline="") as f:
        return f.read()

def wr(p, s):
    with open(p, "w", newline="") as f:
        f.write(s)

def backup(p):
    dst = os.path.join(BK, os.path.basename(p) + ".pre-acer")
    if not os.path.exists(dst):
        shutil.copy2(p, dst)

def fm_set(s, key, value):
    """Set a single-line frontmatter key (inside the first --- block). Skips multi-line values."""
    m = re.match(r"^---\n(.*?)\n---\n", s, re.S)
    if not m:
        return s
    fm = m.group(1)
    if re.search(rf"^{key}:", fm, re.M):
        line = re.search(rf"^{key}:.*$", fm, re.M).group(0)
        if key == "next" and re.match(r"^next:\s*$", line):
            return s
        newfm = re.sub(rf"^{key}:.*$", lambda _m: f"{key}: {value}", fm, count=1, flags=re.M)
    else:
        newfm = fm + f"\n{key}: {value}"
    return "---\n" + newfm + "\n---\n" + s[m.end():]

def patch(path, fn, updated=True, next_=None):
    s = rd(path)
    backup(path)
    s2 = fn(s)
    if updated:
        s2 = fm_set(s2, "updated", DATE)
    if next_:
        s2 = fm_set(s2, "next", '"' + next_ + '"')
    assert "\u2014" not in s2.replace(s, ""), "em dash in new text"
    wr(path, s2)
    print("patched", os.path.basename(path))

def append(s, text):
    return s.rstrip("\n") + "\n\n" + text.strip("\n") + "\n"

def replace_once(s, old, new):
    assert s.count(old) == 1, f"expected one match for: {old[:70]!r} (found {s.count(old)})"
    return s.replace(old, new)

def f_index(s):
    s = replace_once(s, "Builds 1 to 5 are closed on the Dell (2026-10-15), with a short list of items that only you can do; nothing has run on the Acer yet, and its two handoffs (WSL toolkit and Windows toolkit) are ready.==",
        "Builds 1 to 5 are closed on the Dell (2026-10-15). What is left is a short list that only you can do (sudo for socat, UAC prompts for Part 1, one Dell-side read for the host-key check, and decisions).==")
    s = replace_once(s, "| 5b | Acer WSL toolkit sync | **ready, not run** | [[Acer Session Handoff - Toolkit Sync (Build 5b)]] | the handoff note's Results section |",
        "| 5b | Acer WSL toolkit sync | **run 2026-10-15 on the Acer; done except `socat` (needs your sudo)** | [[Acer Session Handoff - Toolkit Sync (Build 5b)]] | the handoff note's Results section |")

    i = s.index("## Acer: what is left (nothing has been run there from this folder)")
    j = s.index("## Order")
    acer = f"""## Acer: what is left (visit of {DATE} done; live-checked on `gupta-builds`)
| # | Item | State | Where it is written |
|---|---|---|---|
| A1 | Build 5b WSL toolkit sync | **DONE** except `socat`. Golden configs applied (`changed=13 same=21 fails=0`), tmux 3.8, pnpm 12.4.2, gh 2.102.0, rg 15.2.0, fd 10.5.0, bat 0.26.1, lazygit 0.66.1, sesh 2.33.0, delta 0.20.1, atuin 18.23.0, uv 0.12.23, ruff, shellcheck, shfmt installed. **You run:** `sudo apt-get update && sudo apt-get install -y socat bubblewrap` (not `31-wsl-sudo-step.sh`: it also disables a WSL Ollama service that does not exist on the Acer and would stop early) | [[Acer Session Handoff - Toolkit Sync (Build 5b)]], Results |
| A2 | Build 2 SSH items | **DONE** except the independent host-key comparison: the Acer's view of the Dell has no `SSH_HostKeys`, so it needs a Dell-side read you run with sudo (command in the Acer Handoff). Passed: Tailscale placement, direct ping, plain `ssh.exe`, `sync-check` over SSH (identical to the Dell), Acer WSL route, clone inventory. Measured: 17 MB bundle in 2.4 s, 512 MiB stream at 2.5 MiB/s (slow, cause unknown, nothing changed) | [[Old Laptop Rebuild - Build 2 Acer Handoff]], "Acer visit results" |
| A3 | Acer audit findings of 2026-10-13 | `.wslconfig` is fixed and live (20 GB, 18 CPUs, 8 GiB swap at `D:\\WSL\\wsl-swap.vhdx`; the old 36 MB `swap.vhdx` is in `D:\\Backup\\Quarantine\\[REDACTED]`); the weekly clean no longer deletes `~/.cache/node`; pnpm back on 12.4.2. **Still open:** WSL is 2.7.14.0 while the Dell is 3.0.3.0 (updating is a host step and your decision); the Ubuntu disk is 40 GiB and 72 percent used; recurring DNS-check journal noise | [[Ubuntu - WSL]], section "Acer toolkit sync and swap fix - 2026-10-15" |
| A4 | Windows toolkit on the Acer | **Part 0 and Part 2 done.** Part 0 found 5 install actions; Part 2 wrote the managed profile blocks, git settings, delta include, Terminal merge, PATH order and two environment variables. **Waiting:** Part 1 (Git and GitHub CLI upgrade with UAC, pnpm 12.11.2, uv Python, mcp-remote) and Part 3 (verify and catalog), then the Dell writes the parity matrix | [[Acer Run Sheet - Windows Toolkit]], section "Acer run log, {DATE}" |
| A5 | **Acer internship clone holds 4 commits that exist nowhere else** (branch `acer-predator/repo-environment-setup`, upstream deleted on GitHub) | protected by a verified Git bundle in `D:\\Backup\\repo-bundles\\`; you decide: push the branch, or bring the commits to the Dell session (the Dell checkout is 6 ahead and 5 behind with 25 dirty files, and its note says the repo is not a uv project while the Acer commits adopt uv) | [[Old Laptop Rebuild - Build 2 Acer Handoff]] |
| A6 | Acer Windows host: Drive backup scope, Drive account question | unchanged | the Drive plan note |
| A7 | Shared bat and fd configs in `Windows Toolkit/configs/` are "reconstructed"; the Acer's own files are the real ones and differ | decide whether to promote the Acer's files into the shared source (then the Dell re-runs `20-apply-configs.ps1`) | [[Acer Run Sheet - Windows Toolkit]] |

"""

# ---------------------------------------------------------------- Run sheet
def f_sheet(s):
    s = append(s, f"""
## Acer run log, {DATE}
Run from the Acer, Windows side, by a Claude Code session started from WSL. Order followed: Part 0, then Part 2. Part 1 and Part 3 are waiting for the user (UAC prompts, closed editors).
**Gotcha that stopped the first attempt:** when the scripts are started from a session or shell that inherited PowerShell 7's `PSModulePath`, Windows PowerShell 5.1 loads PowerShell 7's `Microsoft.PowerShell.Utility` first and every script fails with "The term 'Get-FileHash' is not recognized". Nothing had run. Fix: before starting a script from such a shell, reset the variable: `$env:PSModulePath = [Environment]::GetEnvironmentVariable('PSModulePath','Machine')`. A normal Windows PowerShell 5.1 window does not need it. Not a toolkit bug, but worth a line in `_lib.ps1` (`Initialize-Toolkit`) if you want it to guard itself.
**Part 0 results (read-only):** preflight 47 manifest entries, 5 planned actions; `10 -DryRun` would upgrade Git (2.55.0 to latest) and GitHub CLI (2.101.0 to latest), install pnpm 12.11.2, install a uv-managed Python and install mcp-remote 0.2.1; Node is reported MANUAL because the manifest looks for `OpenJS.NodeJS.22` (the Acer has Node 24.19.0 and the sheet says not to change it). `99-verify` before Part 2: the DIFF rows were versions the Acer is ahead of the Dell lock on (Claude Code 2.1.301, Codex, Cursor, Docker 4.91.0, VS Code 1.142.0, ffmpeg 9.0.1, ripgrep 15.2.0) or behind (pwsh 7.6.7 vs 7.6.6 is ahead; gh 2.101.0, node 24.19.0, npm 11.17.0, Ollama 0.34.1, Antigravity 2.13.0 are behind). Tailscale 1.104.1 PASS.

Tool Catalog - GUPTA-BUILDS.md
[REDACTED].json
tool-catalog-GUPTA-BUILDS.json
function Apply-CopyConfig {
    param($Cfg)
    $src = Join-Path $global:TK.Root $Cfg.source
    $tgt = Expand-ToolkitPath $Cfg.target $global:TK.Vault
    if (-not (Test-Path -LiteralPath $src)) { Add-Result $Cfg.id 'FAIL' ("source missing: {0}" -f $Cfg.source); return }
    if ($Cfg.requiresTool -and -not (Resolve-Cmd $Cfg.requiresTool)) { Add-Result $Cfg.id 'WARN' ("{0} is not installed yet; skipped (run 10-install-toolkit first)" -f $Cfg.requiresTool); return }
    $hits = Get-SecretHitCount ([IO.File]::ReadAllText($src))
    if ($hits -gt 0) { Add-Result $Cfg.id 'FAIL' ("source matches {0} secret pattern(s); not written, text not shown" -f $hits); return }
    $srcHash = Get-FileSha256 $src
    $dstHash = Get-FileSha256 $tgt
    $mode = $Cfg.onExisting.$role
    if ($dstHash -and ($dstHash -eq $srcHash)) {
        Add-Result $Cfg.id 'UNCHANGED' ("{0} already matches the source" -f $tgt) @{ sha256 = $srcHash; target = $tgt }
        $hashRows.Add([pscustomobject]@{ id = $Cfg.id; target = $tgt; source = $srcHash; target_hash = $dstHash; match = $true }); return
    }
    if ($dstHash -and $mode -eq 'keep') {
        Add-Result $Cfg.id 'DIFF' ("kept this machine's own file ({0}); differs from the shared copy by design until promoted" -f $tgt) @{ sha256 = $dstHash; sourceSha256 = $srcHash; target = $tgt }
        $hashRows.Add([pscustomobject]@{ id = $Cfg.id; target = $tgt; source = $srcHash; target_hash = $dstHash; match = $false }); return
    }
    if ($dry) { Add-Result $Cfg.id 'PLAN' ("[DRYRUN] would write {0} (source sha256 {1}...)" -f $tgt, $srcHash.Substring(0, 12)); return }
    $bk = Backup-Target $tgt
    $dir = Split-Path -Parent $tgt
    if (-not (Test-Path -LiteralPath $dir)) { New-Item -ItemType Directory -Force -Path $dir | Out-Null }
    Copy-Item -LiteralPath $src -Destination $tgt -Force
    $new = Get-FileSha256 $tgt
    if ($new -eq $srcHash) { Add-Result $Cfg.id 'CHANGED' ("wrote {0}; hash matches source; backup {1}" -f $tgt, $bk) @{ sha256 = $new; target = $tgt; backup = $bk } }
    else { Add-Result $Cfg.id 'FAIL' ("hash mismatch after copy to {0}" -f $tgt) }
    $hashRows.Add([pscustomobject]@{ id = $Cfg.id; target = $tgt; source = $srcHash; target_hash = $new; match = ($new -eq $srcHash) })
}

B=~/.config/.toolkit-backups/acer-$(date +%Y%m%d-%H%M%S)-bashrc-mcpenv; mkdir -p $B; cp -p ~/.bashrc $B/; echo "backup $B"
python3 -I - <<'EOF'
import os
p=os.path.expanduser('~/.bashrc'); s=open(p).read()
old='[ -f "$HOME/.mcp.env" ] && . "$HOME/.mcp.env"\n'
assert s.count(old)==1
new='''# AI CLIs get ~/.mcp.env (JARVIS_API_KEY, THE_PLAN_API_KEY, GITHUB_PERSONAL_ACCESS_TOKEN) only inside their own process.
# Other shells, tools and children of this shell do not inherit the keys. Add a CLI name to the list to give it the keys.
_ai_run() { local bin; bin=$(type -P "$1") || { echo "$1: not found" >&2; return 127; }; shift
  ( set -a; [ -f "$HOME/.mcp.env" ] && . "$HOME/.mcp.env"; set +a; exec "$bin" "$@" ); }
for _t in claude codex kiro-cli agy opencode; do eval "$_t() { _ai_run $_t \\"\\$@\\"; }"; done; unset _t
'''
open(p,'w').write(s.replace(old,new))
EOF
bash -n ~/.bashrc && echo syntax-ok
echo "== new interactive shell: keys present in env? (names only)"; bash -ic 'env | cut -d= -f1 | grep -E "^(JARVIS_API_KEY|THE_PLAN_API_KEY|GITHUB_PERSONAL_ACCESS_TOKEN)$" || echo "none exported"; type claude | head -1' 2>&1 | grep -v -E 'job control|process group|stty'
echo "== claude still sees MCP servers (through the wrapper)"; timeout 90 bash -ic 'cd /tmp && claude mcp list' 2>&1 | grep -v -E 'job control|process group|stty' | sed -E 's/(Bearer|token)[^ ]*/\1<hidden>/I' | head -14

- `Write` — `/mnt/d/_Anant/20_Progress/Documents/Jarvis/40_Resources/CS/Concepts/New Laptop/Old Laptop Rebuild/WSL Parity/_scripts/70-acer-wsl-update.ps1`
  ```
# 70-acer-wsl-update.ps1 (Acer, 2026-10-15). Updates the WSL platform (not the distro) from Windows PowerShell and verifies the result.
# Run it from Windows PowerShell, NOT from inside WSL: the update shuts WSL down, which ends every WSL session, editor server and Docker Desktop's WSL backend.
# Usage:  powershell.exe -NoProfile -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File D:\WSL\ops\70-acer-wsl-update.ps1            (dry run, changes nothing)
#         powershell.exe -NoProfile -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File D:\WSL\ops\70-acer-wsl-update.ps1 -Apply     (asks YES, then updates)
# ASCII only. PowerShell 5.1 safe. Writes a transcript to D:\WSL\ops\windows\logs\.
param([switch]$Apply, [string]$Distro = 'Ubuntu-24.04')
$ErrorActionPreference = 'Continue'
$env:WSL_UTF8 = '1'
$env:PSModulePath = [Environment]::GetEnvironmentVariable('PSModulePath', 'Machine')
$logDir = 'D:\WSL\ops\windows\logs'
if (-not (Test-Path -LiteralPath $logDir)) { New-Item -ItemType Directory -Force -Path $logDir | Out-Null }
$log = Join-Path $logDir ('70-acer-wsl-update-{0}.log' -f (Get-Date -Format 'yyyyMMdd-HHmmss'))
Start-Transcript -Path $log | Out-Null

function Show-State([string]$label) {
    Write-Host ('--- ' + $label)
    wsl.exe --version
    wsl.exe -l -v
}

Write-Host ('70-acer-wsl-update on ' + $env:COMPUTERNAME + '  apply=' + $Apply.IsPresent + '  distro=' + $Distro)
Show-State 'BEFORE'
Write-Host '--- wslconfig (non-secret keys)'
Get-Content -LiteralPath (Join-Path $env:USERPROFILE '.wslconfig') | ForEach-Object { Write-Host ('  ' + $_) }
$swap = 'D:\WSL\wsl-swap.vhdx'
if (Test-Path -LiteralPath $swap) { Write-Host ('swap file present: ' + $swap) } else { Write-Host ('WARNING: ' + $swap + ' is missing (WSL will create it on start)') }

if (-not $Apply) {
    Write-Host ''
    Write-Host 'DRY RUN: nothing changed. With -Apply this script will:'
    Write-Host '  1. ask you to type YES after you closed VS Code/Cursor windows attached to WSL, Docker Desktop and every WSL terminal'
    Write-Host '  2. run: wsl --update'
    Write-Host '  3. run: wsl --shutdown   (ends this WSL VM; the distro data is untouched)'
    Write-Host ('  4. start ' + $Distro + ' again and check: version, systemd, memory, CPUs, swap, no tailscale in WSL')
    Stop-Transcript | Out-Null
    exit 0
}

$answer = Read-Host 'Everything attached to WSL is closed? Type YES to update WSL'
if ($answer -cne 'YES') { Write-Host 'Cancelled.'; Stop-Transcript | Out-Null; exit 1 }

Write-Host '--- wsl --update'
wsl.exe --update
Write-Host ('update exit code: ' + $LASTEXITCODE)

Write-Host '--- wsl --shutdown (host step; ends the VM)'
wsl.exe --shutdown
Start-Sleep -Seconds 8

Write-Host ('--- starting ' + $Distro)
wsl.exe -d $Distro --exec true
Write-Host ('start exit code: ' + $LASTEXITCODE)
Start-Sleep -Seconds 5
Show-State 'AFTER'

Write-Host '--- checks inside the distro'
wsl.exe -d $Distro --exec bash -c 'echo "systemd: $(systemctl is-system-running)"; echo "failed units: $(systemctl --failed --no-legend | wc -l)"; free -h | sed -n 1,2p; echo "cpus: $(nproc)"; cat /proc/swaps; echo "tailscale in WSL: $(command -v tailscale || echo none)"; df -h / | tail -n 1'
Write-Host '--- tailscale on the Windows side'
tailscale.exe status 2>&1 | Select-Object -First 4

Write-Host ''
Write-Host ('Done. Log: ' + $log)
Write-Host 'Next: open Docker Desktop and your editors again, then tell the Claude session so it can verify and record the result.'
Stop-Transcript | Out-Null

  ```
