# ------------------------------------------------ 1. Build 5b handoff: follow-up
def f_handoff(s):
    s = replace_once(s, "- Items from the table that could not be done, with the exact error: ",
                     "- Items from the table that could not be done, with the exact error (SUPERSEDED, see the follow-up below; socat is now installed): ") if "- Items from the table that could not be done, with the exact error: " in s else s
    return append(s, f"""
### Follow-up the same day, 17:10 to 17:50 CDT
- `socat` installed by the user with `sudo apt-get install -y socat bubblewrap`: socat 1.8.0.0, bubblewrap 0.9.0. The only missing tool is gone; `wsl-check.sh tools` shows no MISSING row.
- Item 7 finished (the `~/.mcp.env` decision): the file holds three variables (`GITHUB_PERSONAL_ACCESS_TOKEN`, `JARVIS_API_KEY`, `THE_PLAN_API_KEY`), read only by Claude Code (`~/.claude.json`) and Codex (`~/.codex/config.toml`). It is no longer sourced into every shell. Instead `~/.bashrc` defines small wrapper functions for `claude`, `codex`, `kiro-cli`, `agy` and `opencode` that load `~/.mcp.env` inside that one process only (`_ai_run`). Result from a clean `env -i` interactive shell: none of the three keys is exported; through the wrapper `claude mcp list` shows `jarvis`, `the-plan` and `jarvis-fs` connected and `codex mcp list` lists `github`, `jarvis` and `jarvis-fs`. To give another CLI the keys, add its name to the `for _t in ...` list. This block is Acer-local (not in the golden set); the Dell can adopt the same block if wanted. Backup `~/.config/.toolkit-backups/[REDACTED]`.
- WSL platform update (2.7.14.0 to the Dell's 3.0.3.0) is a host step: `WSL Parity/_scripts/70-acer-wsl-update.ps1` (copy at `D:\\WSL\\ops\\70-acer-wsl-update.ps1`). The dry run was executed and changed nothing; the update itself waits for the user (command in the Index, Acer ledger A3).
- WSL disk inventory taken for the planned WSL-role discussion (read-only, nothing removed): `/` 39 GiB with 11 GiB free; `~/.cache` 7.7 GB (uv 7.5 GB, regenerable), `.vscode-server` 4.1 GB, `miniconda3` 3.8 GB, `.codex` 2.1 GB, `.local` 1.8 GB, `.rustup` 1.5 GB, `.npm` 0.9 GB, `.nvm` 0.7 GB, `.cargo` 0.5 GB, `projects` 252 MB (of which `scratch/gpu-smoke-test` is 5.3 GB on disk before ignore rules, `ai` 211 MB, `work` 41 MB). `ext4.vhdx` is 28.9 GiB on D:.
""")
patch(f"{OLR}/WSL Parity/Acer Session Handoff - Toolkit Sync (Build 5b).md", f_handoff,
      next_="Build 5b is complete on the Acer. Nothing left to run from this note. The WSL role discussion follows.")

# ------------------------------------------------ 2. Build 2 Acer handoff: host key command
def f_b2h(s):
    old = "in Dell WSL run `sudo find /var/lib/tailscale -iname '*ssh_host*'` and then `sudo ssh-keygen -lf <the .pub file or the private key path it prints>`, and compare with the fingerprint above."
    new = "in Dell WSL run `sudo ssh-keygen -lf /var/lib/tailscale/ssh/ssh_host_ed25519_key` (the file exists; the user listed `ssh_host_ed25519_key`, `ssh_host_rsa_key` and `ssh_host_ecdsa_key` there on 2026-10-15) and compare the printed `SHA256:` value with the fingerprint above. Type the real path; the first attempt pasted the placeholder `<path ...>` and bash rejected it."
    return replace_once(s, old, new)
patch(f"{OLR}/Old Laptop Rebuild - Build 2 Acer Handoff.md", f_b2h)

Remaining DIFF rows are version drift against the Dell lock (Acer ahead: pwsh 7.6.7, ripgrep 15.2.0, ffmpeg 9.0.1, Claude Code 2.1.301, Codex 0.162.1, Cursor 3.24.9, Docker 4.91.0, VS Code 1.142.0; Acer behind: Node 24.19.0 vs 24.20.0, npm 11.17.0 vs 11.19.0, Ollama 0.34.1 vs 0.40.3, Antigravity 2.13.0 vs 2.22.0) and `cfg-bat`, `cfg-fd-ignore`. `40-build-catalog.ps1` PASS: `catalog/Tool Catalog - GUPTA-BUILDS.md` and `tool-catalog-GUPTA-BUILDS.json` (41 manifest tools). - **bat and fd promoted (done):** the Acer's real files were copied byte for byte into `configs/bat/config` and `configs/fd/ignore` (SHA-256 `F613417D...` and `6A549EAF...`, no secret-like text). # ------------------------------------------------ 5. Locked decisions: packageManager register
LD = f"{NL}/Codebases/Codebases - Two-Laptop SSH Workflow - Locked Decisions and Postmortem.md"
def f_ld(s):
    return append(s, f"""
## packageManager register ({DATE}, read-only audit of the Dell's canonical repos)
Decision 11 and the Build 5b decision on pnpm 12.4.2 mean a pin belongs in each repo's `package.json`, but only where the repo really uses that manager. The audit found that a blanket `pnpm@12.4.2` would be wrong for most repos. Nothing was changed in any repo. Each row is a separate, per-repo approval, done in its own `<machine>/pin-package-manager` branch or worktree, one repo at a time, and only when the working tree is clean enough to review the diff.
| Repo (under `~/projects`) | Lockfiles found | `packageManager` today | Proposed pin | Blocker or note |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `hub/portfolio` | `pnpm-lock.yaml` | none | `pnpm@12.4.2` | 114 dirty files on `post-frontend`; pin only after that work is committed or moved |
| `hub/Learning-Tracker-Tool` | `pnpm-lock.yaml` | none | `pnpm@12.4.2` | 2 dirty files; clean candidate for the first pilot |
| `hub/Assisto_website` | `pnpm-lock.yaml` and `package-lock.json` (mixed) | none | `pnpm@12.4.2` after deleting the stale `package-lock.json` | 73 dirty files; choose one manager first |
| `hackathon/Resq`, `hackathon/opspilot`, `hackathon/safereach`, `hub/DNA_BJJ_APP`, `hub/GymMangment_app_demo` | `package-lock.json` | none | `npm@11.x` (match the Node 24 npm) | npm repos: do not pin pnpm; pin npm only if you want Corepack to enforce it |
| `hub/boom-tracer` | `package-lock.json` and `bun.lockb` (mixed) | none | decide npm or bun first | mixed lockfiles |
| `ai/claude/ecc` | `package-lock.json` and `yarn.lock` | `yarn@4.9.2` (upstream clone) | leave | upstream's pin; do not edit |
| `ai/jan` | `yarn.lock` | `yarn@4.5.3` (upstream clone) | leave | upstream's pin; do not edit |
| `hub/CausalOps`, `hub/tradingview`, `work/internship-research-loop`, `ai/claude/second-brain-claudekit`, `work/gupta-builds`, `ai/claude/adx-worktree-throwaway-test` | none (no `package.json`) | n/a | none | Python or docs repos; they need a Python pin (`.python-version`, `uv.lock`) instead, not a Node one |
| `umn/boom` | `Cargo.lock` | n/a | none | Rust |
Acer clones: only `second-brain-claudekit` and `internship-research-loop` exist as shared clones there, and neither has a `package.json`. The Acer's internship clone already commits `pyproject.toml` and `uv.lock` (four unpushed commits, see [[Old Laptop Rebuild - Build 2 Acer Handoff]]); the Dell's checkout has neither, so that repo's Python pin needs one decision across both laptops before any build touches it. Remember `~/.cache/node` must never be deleted: Corepack reads its pin from there.
""")
patch(LD, f_ld)

def f_inst(s):

                     "| socat | 1.8.0.0 | installed by the user with `sudo apt-get install -y socat bubblewrap` (2026-10-15 17:15) |")
    s = replace_once(s, "Tailscale is not installed in this WSL (it runs on Windows only).",
                     "The AI CLIs (`claude`, `codex`, `kiro-cli`, `agy`, `opencode`) are wrapper functions that load `~/.mcp.env` inside their own process only; no other shell or tool inherits the three keys. Tailscale is not installed in this WSL (it runs on Windows only).")
    s = replace_once(s, "Part 1 still has to install: Git and GitHub CLI upgrades (UAC), pnpm 12.11.2 (Windows copy), uv-managed Python and mcp-remote. Its bat and fd files are the real ones and differ from the shared reconstructed copies (kept by design).",
                     "Part 1 then ran (user, UAC): pnpm 12.11.2 (Windows copy), uv-managed Python 3.13.15 and mcp-remote 0.2.1 installed, GitHub CLI upgraded to 2.102.0. Part 3 ran: `99-verify.ps1` has 0 FAIL, the catalog is `Tool Catalog - GUPTA-BUILDS`. The Acer's real bat and fd files were promoted into the shared toolkit source; the Dell re-runs `20-apply-configs.ps1` once to match. Open on this side: Node 24.19.0 (Dell 24.20.0) is a user decision.")
    s = replace_once(s, f"### Acer, Windows side ({DATE}: Part 0 and Part 2 of the run sheet done; Part 1 and Part 3 waiting)",
                     f"### Acer, Windows side ({DATE}: Parts 0 to 3 of the run sheet done)")
    return s
patch(f"{NL}/Installations.md", f_inst)

## Claude

That error is useful: Tailscale's host key file isn't in OpenSSH format, so `ssh-keygen` can't read it. The fingerprint can still be computed from the key's public part. Before giving you a command, I'm testing the method on a throwaway key, converted to the same non-OpenSSH format, to prove it matches `ssh-keygen`.

That Dell run was useful: `ssh-keygen` can't read Tailscale's host key file, so the check needed a different method. It is fixed now, and you can run the WSL update whenever you like.

## Host-key check
Tailscale stores that key as a plain `BEGIN PRIVATE KEY` file, which `ssh-keygen -lf` rejects. I tested a different method on a throwaway key in the same format, and it gave exactly the same fingerprint as `ssh-keygen`. It derives only the public half and prints only the fingerprint, never the key. I saved it as a script in the vault, so it can't be mangled by pasting. It reaches the Dell by Syncthing; if the file isn't there yet, use the one-liner below it.

In Dell WSL:
```bash
sudo bash "/mnt/d/Users/_Anant/10_Areas/Documents/Jarvis/40_Resources/CS/Concepts/New Laptop/Old Laptop Rebuild/WSL Parity/_scripts/71-hostkey-fingerprint.sh"
```

If the file hasn't synced yet, this does the same without it:
```bash
sudo openssl pkey -in /var/lib/tailscale/ssh/ssh_host_ed25519_key -pubout -outform DER | tail -c 32 | { printf '\0\0\0\x0bssh-ed25519\0\0\0\x20'; cat; } | openssl dgst -sha256 -binary | base64 | tr -d '=' | sed 's/^/SHA256:/'
```
The Acer accepted `SHA256:[REDACTED]`. The script prints MATCH or DIFFERENT. If it says DIFFERENT, don't accept any other key, and tell me.

- The Build 2 Acer Handoff, the SSH Runbook and the Index previously told you to use `ssh-keygen -lf`, which fails here. They now point to the script and explain why. The scripts README lists it too.

  ```bash
P="/mnt/d/_Anant/20_Progress/Documents/Jarvis/40_Resources/CS/Concepts/New Laptop/Old Laptop Rebuild"
cat > "$P/WSL Parity/_scripts/71-hostkey-fingerprint.sh" <<'EOF'
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# 71-hostkey-fingerprint.sh (2026-10-15). Run on the DELL in WSL:  sudo bash 71-hostkey-fingerprint.sh
# Prints the SHA256 fingerprint of Tailscale SSH's ed25519 host key, so it can be compared with the one the Acer accepted.
# Why this exists: Tailscale stores the key as a plain PKCS8 PEM ("BEGIN PRIVATE KEY"), which ssh-keygen -lf rejects ("is not a key file").
# Only the PUBLIC half is derived and hashed; the private key is never printed. Method verified against ssh-keygen on a test key.
set -euo pipefail
[ "$(id -u)" -eq 0 ] || { echo "Run with sudo: sudo bash $0"; exit 1; }
KEY=/var/lib/tailscale/ssh/ssh_host_ed25519_key
echo "key file header: $(head -n 1 "$KEY")"
FP=$(openssl pkey -in "$KEY" -pubout -outform DER | tail -c 32 | { printf '\0\0\0\x0bssh-ed25519\0\0\0\x20'; cat; } | openssl dgst -sha256 -binary | base64 | tr -d '=')
echo "Dell host key fingerprint : SHA256:$FP"
echo "Fingerprint the Acer holds: SHA256:PHOUaZNkip9YvF80hW6WbG5emclHt7rIYMfWf3VzxHA"
if [ "SHA256:$FP" = "SHA256:PHOUaZNkip9YvF80hW6WbG5emclHt7rIYMfWf3VzxHA" ]; then echo "RESULT: MATCH"; else echo "RESULT: DIFFERENT (stop; do not accept another key)"; exit 2; fi
EOF
bash -n "$P/WSL Parity/_scripts/71-hostkey-fingerprint.sh" && echo syntax-ok; LC_ALL=C grep -cP '[^\x00-\x7F]' "$P/WSL Parity/_scripts/71-hostkey-fingerprint.sh"

WSL went from 2.7.14.0 to 3.0.3.0, the same version as the Dell, and the kernel from 6.18.33.2 to 6.18.54.1. The script's `systemd:` line printed blank because it checked too soon after the restart, so I verified it myself.

import os, re, shutil
ROOT = "/mnt/d/_Anant/20_Progress/Documents/Jarvis"
NL = f"{ROOT}/40_Resources/CS/Concepts/New Laptop"
OLR = f"{NL}/Old Laptop Rebuild"
BK = f"{OLR}/Runs/_backups/acer-visit-20261015"
os.makedirs(BK, exist_ok=True)
DATE = "2026-10-15"
def rd(p):
    with open(p, newline="") as f: return f.read()
def wr(p, s):
    with open(p, "w", newline="") as f: f.write(s)
def backup(p):
    dst = os.path.join(BK, os.path.basename(p) + ".pre-acer")
    if not os.path.exists(dst): shutil.copy2(p, dst)
def fm_set(s, key, value):
    m = re.match(r"^---\n(.*?)\n---\n", s, re.S)
    if not m: return s
    fm = m.group(1)
    if re.search(rf"^{key}:", fm, re.M):
        line = re.search(rf"^{key}:.*$", fm, re.M).group(0)
        if key == "next" and re.match(r"^next:\s*$", line): return s
        newfm = re.sub(rf"^{key}:.*$", lambda _m: f"{key}: {value}", fm, count=1, flags=re.M)
    else:
        newfm = fm + f"\n{key}: {value}"
    return "---\n" + newfm + "\n---\n" + s[m.end():]
def patch(path, fn, updated=True, next_=None):
    s = rd(path); backup(path); s2 = fn(s)
    if updated: s2 = fm_set(s2, "updated", DATE)
    if next_: s2 = fm_set(s2, "next", '"' + next_ + '"')
    assert s2.count("—") <= s.count("—"), "em dash in new text"
    wr(path, s2); print("patched", os.path.basename(path))
def append(s, text): return s.rstrip("\n") + "\n\n" + text.strip("\n") + "\n"
def replace_once(s, old, new):
    assert s.count(old) == 1, f"expected one match for: {old[:70]!r} (found {s.count(old)})"
    return s.replace(old, new)

## Claude

This is a design discussion, and the answers depend on what your notes already say, so I'm reading first and changing nothing. I'm starting with the Codebases folder you pointed to, and then searching the vault for how conversation capture and session hooks actually work.

Decision 11 already answers part of your question, so I'll build on it. Now the per-repo notes: the second-brain-claudekit set first, since that's where conversation tracking lives.

Next are the three internship notes and the two VS Code home notes (`wsl-home` and `windows-home`), since they describe what lives in the home directories.

I changed nothing for this discussion; I read the Codebases notes and inspected both machines. Several answers come from what is actually on the machines, and two of them turned up faults that need fixing before the Remote-SSH design works.

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
- A tool's state follows the machine where its process runs, not where you sit. A session driven from the Acer through Remote-SSH runs on the Dell's WSL, so it uses the Dell's Claude settings, hooks, toolchain, caches and transcripts. The Acer only draws the screen.
- That makes the Dell the only machine whose capture hooks matter for shared-repo work. Right now they are broken (see below).
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

## 1. The Tailscale setup, step by step
| Piece | Where it runs | State |
|---|---|---|
| Tailscale node `dell-wsl` (100.94.129.113), SSH server (`RunSSH`), key expiry off | inside the Dell's WSL | working |
| Tailscale node `gupta-builds` (100.84.91.11), client only | Acer Windows | working |
| `ssh.exe` and VS Code Remote-SSH client | Acer Windows | working, host key verified (MATCH) |
| VS Code Server, extensions, shells, Claude, Codex, git, node, uv | Dell WSL | all of the "work" happens here |
| Acer WSL | Acer | no Tailscale, no role in this path |

The path is Acer VS Code → Windows `ssh.exe` → Windows Tailscale → direct WireGuard (4 to 13 ms) → Tailscale SSH inside the Dell's WSL. Tailscale SSH asks for a browser "check" now and then, which is a one-time click that a non-interactive run will wait on.

The weak point is the Dell after a reboot. WSL, Tailscale and Obsidian only come up after someone signs in. Auto sign-in was rejected because of BitLocker, and nothing auto-starts since Build 4b. After an unplanned Dell reboot, the Acer cannot connect until you sign in at the Dell. This should be fixed in Build 7.

## 2. Your questions
**Claude home settings.** There are four separate `.claude` homes, and the one used is the one on the machine running the process:
- A Remote-SSH session (the VS Code extension, or `claude` in an `ssh dell-wsl` tab) uses the **Dell WSL** home: its hooks, skills, deny rules and MCP config. That home holds 136 transcripts, 1.9 GB of skills and 618 MB of plugins.
- `claude` typed in the Acer's PowerShell uses the Acer Windows home (216 transcripts, 552 MB).
- Acer WSL (21 transcripts, 42 MB) is only used if you work inside it.

**Terminal settings.** The Acer draws everything and the Dell runs everything. Fonts, the Windows Terminal scheme and the VS Code UI are the Acer's. The shell, `.bashrc`, starship, tmux, yazi and atuin are the Dell's. One unverified wrinkle: tmux copy goes through `win32yank.exe`, which would fill the Dell's clipboard, not the Acer's. The golden config uses OSC 52 (`set-clipboard external`), which may work in the Acer's terminals; that needs testing.

**Packages.** For Remote-SSH work the toolchain is entirely the Dell's. The Acer WSL toolkit we just built only matters for Acer-local work.

**Caches.** They land where the process runs: node_modules, `.venv`, pnpm, uv and npm caches, `~/.vscode-server`, Docker images and git objects are all on the Dell. The Acer keeps only its client-side bits (VS Code UI state, Terminal, `known_hosts`, Tailscale state, Claude Desktop). So the disk pressure moves to the Dell, which already has the weekly clean and daily watcher timers, and the rule to never delete `~/.cache/node` now applies to both of you.

**Conversations and hooks.** The pipeline is Stop and SessionEnd hooks that run a `pwsh` export script, plus scheduled backfills, writing into the vault's `AI Conversations`. Remote-SSH sessions write their transcripts under the **Dell's** `~/.claude/projects`, and the Dell's hooks should capture them. What I found:
1. **Dell WSL capture is probably silently broken.** The Dell's hook (dated Aug 11) writes to `AI Conversations/WSL/Claude Code`, a folder that no longer exists. The vault now has `Dell/`, `Acer/` and `Windows/` instead, and the script quietly returns when the folder is missing. The newest `Dell/WSL` capture is 2026-10-13. I haven't run the hook to prove it, so treat it as high confidence, not certain.
2. **The Acer has no capture at all.** There are no global hooks on Acer Windows or WSL, no capture tasks, and `AI Conversations/Acer` is empty.
3. **The shared export script isn't machine-aware.** It writes to a single `Windows/` folder. Both laptops' Windows sessions would mix there, and its junction to `_raw_jsonl` is machine-local.
4. **Cursor over SSH won't route.** The pipeline joins JSONL (on the Dell) with SQLite (on the Acer's Windows), and its routing only knows `wsl+` URIs, not `ssh-remote`.
5. **A risk to test:** `~/.mcp.env` is loaded only by interactive shells on the Dell. The VS Code extension host under Remote-SSH may not inherit those keys, so Jarvis MCP could fail in extension-driven sessions while working in terminal ones. The Dell's Obsidian must also be running, since `127.0.0.1:27123` resolves on the Dell.

**The two Acer clones.**
- `second-brain-claudekit`: clean, 0 ahead and 0 behind, no unique work. Its Unison sync to the Jarvis mirror must run from one checkout only (the Dell's), or you get a second writer into Syncthing. I'd back it up as a bundle and remove it, since a re-clone is 211 MB.
- `internship-research-loop`: its 4 local-only commits are the Acer's uv conversion, which matches your Environment Rule ("everything else: a uv project"). They should go to GitHub as a PR from `acer-predator/repo-environment-setup` and be merged with the Dell's branch, which is 6 ahead and 5 behind. Then the clone can go the same way.
- The `second-brain-claudekit` and `internship-research-loop` bootstrap notes describe cloning on the Acer as a migration target. That is superseded by Decisions 4, 5 and 11.

## 3. What the Acer's WSL is for
Three jobs remain, and nothing else belongs there:
1. **GPU work.** The Dell has no GPU and the Acer has the RTX 5070 Ti. This covers CUDA, local models, Docker with GPU, and Acer-only repos like `gpu-smoke-test` (5.3 GB).
2. **The fallback** when the Dell is off: clone from GitHub and push `acer-predator/<topic>` branches (Decision 9).
3. **Quick local Linux commands.**

Shared repos, sync scripts and capture of shared work do not belong there.

**The 39 GiB disk.** I'd resize nothing now. About 7.5 GB of uv cache and about 4 GB of VS Code Server are regenerable, which gives roughly 22 GiB free. If GPU data needs more, growing the virtual disk is non-destructive, but I'd verify the exact `wsl --manage` syntax first. Take a `wsl --export` before any destructive change; the Acer has none yet and D: has about 518 GB free.

**The slow transfer.** I measured 2.5 MiB/s on a 512 MiB stream. The candidates are Tailscale SSH's Go implementation being single-stream, the Dell's 1280 MTU, and my test going through WSL mirrored networking. A proper test would repeat it from Windows `ssh.exe`, add a raw TCP test, and watch the Dell's CPU. It barely affects interactive editing, so it ranks below the capture fix.

**Windows pieces that would smooth Remote-SSH.** Each needs your approval, and some VS Code settings sync to the Dell, so I'd check their scope first.
- An SSH config alias with keep-alives.
- Tailscale on the Acer set to start unattended before login.
- `remote.SSH.*` VS Code settings, such as default extensions.
- A Windows Terminal profile and a `code --remote ssh-remote+dell-wsl <path>` shortcut.
- On the Dell: coming up after a reboot without anyone signing in.

# Codebases - Two-Laptop SSH Workflow - Locked Decisions and Postmortem

## One-Line Answer
The Dell stays the canonical host and Tailscale stays inside WSL only (never on either Windows side) — that part of Prompt 2 was already right — but Prompt 2's own stay-awake risk is currently unresolved by any setting either prompt actually sets, Tailscale SSH should be primary (not OpenSSH+keys) with OpenSSH kept only as fallback, and both repos' real, dated, dual-checkout branch model has to be explicitly retired, not silently outgrown.

## Status
Supersedes the codebase-sync "Verdict" section of `60_Claude/40_Project_Briefs/Codebase Sync Decision and Logging Failure Audit — 2026-10-04.md` (that note's separate logging-audit half is untouched and still current). This note does not rewrite [[Old Laptop Rebuild - Prompt 2 VS Code and Two-Laptop Workflow]] — it is the input the rewrite has to carry. Every external claim below was checked live against current Tailscale and Microsoft documentation on 2026-10-04, not carried over from either Prompt 2's own text or the prior review session's assumptions — two of the ten decisions below correct something both of those got wrong or left unresolved.

---

## Decision 1 — Tailscale goes inside WSL only. This was already correct; verify it, don't re-decide it.
> [!IMPORTANT] Amended 2026-10-04 by the user
> The rule is now **one location per machine, never both**. The Dell runs Tailscale inside WSL only (the Tailscale SSH server exists only on Linux and macOS, and the code lives in WSL). The Acer, a client only, runs Tailscale on its **Windows side only** and never inside its WSL. Reason: VS Code Remote-SSH on the Acer uses the Windows `ssh.exe`, which needs a Windows-side tailnet route, and Tailscale's WSL2 page recommends the Windows host alone for ordinary use while warning only about running both on one machine. Windows-side Tailscale moves no code or tooling out of WSL. The text below was written before this amendment; where it says "never on either Windows side", read it as "never on the Dell's Windows side, and never in the Acer's WSL". See [[Old Laptop Rebuild - Prompt 2 VS Code and Two-Laptop Workflow]], rule R2.

Prompt 2's locked design already says Tailscale and sshd run inside the WSL distro, with its own tailnet name (`dell-wsl`), never on either laptop's Windows side. **Keep this.** The prior review session's suggestion to add this as a standing rule is right — but confirm the reasoning precisely, because the real failure mode is narrower than "never run it on both":

- Tailscale's own install docs (`tailscale.com/docs/install/windows/wsl2`, last validated 2025-11-03) state the actual failure: running Tailscale on **both** the Windows host and inside WSL2 **at the same time** breaks outbound WSL traffic, because WSL's own Tailscale-encrypted packets get wrapped a second time going out through the Windows host's Tailscale tunnel and no longer fit (an MTU/double-encapsulation failure, not a config mistake you can tune around). Running Tailscale in WSL *alone*, with Windows never running it, is a supported configuration — the danger is specifically the combination, not the WSL side on its own.
- Not yet checked by either Prompt 2 or the prior review: that warning is written for WSL2 in general, not specifically for `networkingMode=mirrored` (the Dell's actual mode, confirmed in Prompt 2's own facts). **Phase 1 must add a live round-trip connectivity test** (ping + an actual SSH connection from a second device, not just `tailscale status`), and confirm the documented MTU workaround fires: the same docs say `tailscaled` auto-raises WSL's default-interface MTU from 1280 to 1340 when it detects WSL — verify this happened (`ip link show` inside WSL after `tailscale up`), don't assume it.
- **Standing rule to add next to "never run `wsl --shutdown` mid-session":** Tailscale is never installed on either laptop's Windows side, full stop. Record this in Prompt 2's rewrite as a named rule, not an inference from "the design doesn't mention it."

## Decision 2 — Tailscale SSH primary, keyed OpenSSH kept only as fallback
Prompt 2 currently plans key-only OpenSSH as the only SSH mechanism. Change the default to Tailscale SSH, with the already-planned OpenSSH server demoted to fallback — this is not extra work, since Prompt 2 was already going to install an OpenSSH server; only the primary/fallback ordering changes.

**Why, confirmed directly from Tailscale's own docs (`tailscale.com/docs/features/tailscale-ssh`):**
- Tailscale SSH authenticates via the tailnet's own node identity, not a distributed key pair — it intercepts SSH traffic through `netstack` port interception and the SSH protocol's `none` auth type once `tailscaled` already knows who the remote peer is. No key generation, no `authorized_keys`, no key rotation.
- **Zero client-side config.** Confirmed directly (a Tailscale engineer, on the record): "Client changes aren't needed for this to work, all that is required is to use your SSH client as normal." VS Code's Remote-SSH extension needs nothing extra — it just calls `ssh host` the normal way, and Tailscale's daemon on the target machine takes it from there. This is a real simplification over the pasted review's assumption that this needed special VS Code config.
- **Host-key churn is a non-issue under Tailscale SSH specifically** — this resolves one of the prior review's named landmines, not just works around it. Tailscale distributes the SSH host public key through its own control plane, separately from node keys, specifically so a reinstalled WSL distro's new host key propagates automatically and the client never sees an "unknown host" warning. (Host-key churn is still a real manual fix — `ssh-keygen -R <host>` on the Acer — if you ever fall back to the plain OpenSSH path.)
- **The one real limitation, stated explicitly in Tailscale's own docs, to accept rather than "fix":** Tailscale SSH doesn't check a client-side key pair, so *any* OS user on the connecting machine with tailnet access can SSH in as the configured WSL user. For two personal devices with one user account each, this is the correct tradeoff to accept explicitly, not a gap to patch.
- **Unresolved, needs a live check in Phase 4, not an assumption:** whether Tailscale SSH authorizes the connection by default for a personal (non-org) tailnet with no ACL file, or needs an explicit `ssh` block added to the tailnet policy — sources disagree by year (a 2022 comment from Tailscale's own co-founder says personal tailnets default to allowing SSH to your own untagged devices; a more recent third-party guide says an explicit `ssh` ACL rule is always required with no default). **Check the admin console's Access Controls page directly before assuming either answer; don't ship a prompt that asserts one.**
- Keep the already-planned sshd hardening file (`PasswordAuthentication no`, `PermitRootLogin no`, `AllowUsers anant_gupta`, `AllowTcpForwarding yes`) — but note explicitly in the rewrite that this file only matters for the **fallback** path. If Tailscale SSH is primary and healthy, sshd's own auth settings are bypassed entirely by `tailscale up --ssh`'s interception; don't let Phase 4 think the hardening work was wasted if it's rarely exercised.

## Decision 3 — the actual stay-awake fix, which neither prompt currently sets
**This is the single most important correction from this research pass.** Prompt 2 names "stay-awake risk" as real and explicitly defers the fix to Prompt 1 ("The WSL idle timeouts live in `.wslconfig`, which Build 1 owns"). But Prompt 1's own target-state list for `.wslconfig` (`memory`, `processors`, `swap`, `swapfile`, `networkingMode`, `firewall`, `autoMemoryReclaim`, `sparseVhd`) **does not include any idle-timeout setting at all.** The handoff between the two prompts drops the one setting the whole design depends on.

Confirmed directly against Microsoft's own WSL config docs (`learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/wsl/wsl-config`) and a live, dated (April 2026) community report of the exact failure mode:
- There are **two separate timers**, not one: `vmIdleTimeout` (default 60000ms / 60s) under `[wsl2]` shuts down the whole lightweight VM; `instanceIdleTimeout` (default 15000ms / **15 seconds**) under `[general]` shuts down the specific distro instance independently of the VM timer.
- Setting only `vmIdleTimeout=-1` — which is the one setting anyone usually finds first — **does not work on its own.** Directly confirmed by a user who had it set and still watched their distro (with systemd and background services running) die ~15 seconds after the last terminal session closed: "that doesn't work... it does not prevent your distribution from stopping after the last terminal closes, even with systemd running." The fix needs **both**:
  ```ini
  # In %UserProfile%\.wslconfig
  [wsl2]
  vmIdleTimeout=-1

  [general]
  instanceIdleTimeout=-1
  ```
- This directly resolves Prompt 2's own flagged unresolved question ("whether sshd-as-a-systemd-service... keeps the distro alive indefinitely... flags this as unverified, correctly"). The verified answer is **no, an active sshd service does not keep the distro alive by itself** — the 15-second `instanceIdleTimeout` kills the distro regardless of what's running inside it, unless this setting is explicitly disabled.
- **Tradeoff to accept explicitly, not silently:** `-1` on both means the Dell's WSL VM and distro never auto-shut down to reclaim resources, ever — this is the deliberate cost of being an always-on SSH host, not a side effect to discover later. `autoMemoryReclaim=gradual` (already in Prompt 1's target state) is still worth keeping alongside this — it reclaims idle memory *while the VM stays running*, which is compatible with, not contradicted by, disabling the two shutdown timers.
- **Add to Prompt 1's target state, item 1**, and remove the implicit assumption from Prompt 2's "Stay-awake risk" paragraph that this is simply "recorded" for Build 1 — Build 1's own prompt text needs the explicit setting, or the same gap recurs.
- **Verification Prompt 1 or Prompt 2's Phase 1 must run, live, not assumed from the docs:** close every WSL terminal and VS Code window, wait at least 30 seconds, then confirm from PowerShell (`wsl -l -v` should still show the distro `Running`) and confirm sshd still answers a connection. Do this before trusting any later phase of the two-laptop design.

## Decision 4 — retire the existing dual-checkout model explicitly; don't assume Prompt 2 is a continuation of it

> [!NOTE] Build 5 correction, 2026-10-13
> The Dell's second-brain checkout is `~/projects/ai/claude/second-brain-claudekit` (the Acer uses `ai/second-brain-claudekit`) and it has no root package manifest. The internship-research-loop checkout is not a live uv project (no `pyproject.toml`).

Both `second-brain-claudekit` and `internship-research-loop` already have a real, dated, working model that Prompt 2 silently replaces, not extends:
- `internship-research-loop-new-laptop-directive.md` (2026-09-26): each laptop holds its **own independent clone**, branches named `dell-latitude/<topic>` / `acer-predator/<topic>`, merged by PR, documented in the repo's own `CLAUDE.md` under "Two-laptop workflow" (added 2026-09-26, in the Auto-mode classifier notes section).
- **Important and easy to miss: the vault's own mirrored copy of that `CLAUDE.md`** (`20_Progress/AI/Claude Code/internship-research-loop/CLAUDE.md`) **is stale and does not contain this section at all** — its last write is 2026-09-05, three weeks before the section was added to the real repo. This is a live, concrete example of the one-way-mirror risk already flagged in the superseded brief: **don't trust the vault's mirrored `CLAUDE.md` copies as source of truth for either repo's current two-laptop conventions — read the live WSL checkout directly.**
- Prompt 2's own "Verified facts" section confirms the dual-checkout model is "already in use" today (18 repos on Dell, `<machine>/<topic>` branch convention, GitHub-only) — so the single-canonical-host design is a real, second architecture change layered on top of a model that's currently active, not a greenfield decision.

**Add to Prompt 2's rewrite, Phase 4 (Acer handoff), as an explicit step:** inventory the Acer's existing clones of both repos and either retire or clearly relabel them before the handoff completes. An old, no-longer-authoritative local clone left sitting on the Acer's disk is the same shape of failure the vault's own sync history already caught once — [[Cross-Laptop Sync - Build 4 Findings]] found a live, forgotten Next.js checkout (`.git`, `node_modules`, `.env.local` intact) sitting inside a folder nobody remembered was a real codebase.

## Decision 5 — both repos' completion-gate checklists need a dated rewrite note, not silent obsolescence
Read directly from both directive notes' "Final completion gate" sections:
- `internship-research-loop`'s gate includes `gh auth status succeeds without exposing a token` and `First new work on the new laptop starts on an acer-predator/<topic> branch, not master` — both assume the Acer runs its own independent `gh`-authenticated clone. Under the single-host design, the Acer never runs `gh` or holds git credentials at all; every push/PR executes on the Dell via the Remote-SSH session. This gate item becomes meaningless, not merely redundant, and will read as a false regression to a future audit unless it's explicitly marked superseded.
- `second-brain-claudekit`'s gate has no equivalent laptop-specific branch or `gh auth` item — its checks are tool/sync-presence checks (`git lfs version`, `unison -version`, the sync script exit code), which stay valid either way. **No rewrite needed there** — don't assume symmetry between the two repos' gates just because they're handled by the same prompt.

**Add to Prompt 2's rewrite:** a step that patches `internship-research-loop-new-laptop-directive.md`'s completion gate with a dated note ("superseded by the single-host design, see [[Codebases - Two-Laptop SSH Workflow - Locked Decisions and Postmortem]]") rather than leaving it to look like an unmet checklist item indefinitely.

## Decision 6 — the Jarvis and The Plan vaults are out of scope; say so, don't imply it
Prompt 2's scope is `~/projects` and the 18 repos under it. The vaults already have a working, different, deliberately-opposite-philosophy sync mechanism — Syncthing's real-time mirror plus `Jarvis-GitAutoSync`'s scheduled commit/push, Builds 0-11 — built specifically because independent live copies (what the vaults need) and one-canonical-checkout (what Prompt 2 is building for code) solve different problems. Layering Prompt 2's model onto the vaults would fight that design directly, not complement it.

**Add one explicit line to Prompt 2's rewrite:** "This build's scope is `~/projects` only. The Jarvis and The Plan vaults keep their existing Syncthing + git-auto-sync mechanism unchanged; do not touch `.stignore`, `.gitignore`, or either vault's Scheduled Tasks from this build." Don't rely on scope being implied by the reading list.

## Decision 7 — worktree dependency caches: the specific settings, confirmed

> [!NOTE] Build 5 correction, 2026-10-13
> pnpm 10.33.2 uses the key `enableGlobalVirtualStore`, not `virtualStoreType: global`. Only set it on repos that really use pnpm, and measure first.
> Update 2026-10-14: both laptops now run pnpm 12.4.2 (user matched the Acer). The key above was verified for 10.33.2 only; on 12.4.2 `pnpm config get` shows neither key set, so check pnpm.io before setting any global-virtual-store option.

Both repos' package managers already support exactly this scenario natively — this needs a specific setting, not just an awareness that "caches help":
- **`second-brain-claudekit` (pnpm):** pnpm's own documentation (`pnpm.io/git-worktrees`, `pnpm.io/global-virtual-store`) describes this exact use case by name ("pnpm + Git Worktrees for Multi-Agent Development"). Default pnpm already hardlinks package contents from a shared content-addressable store into each project's `node_modules/.pnpm` — real bytes aren't duplicated, but the hardlink *directory structure* is still rebuilt per worktree, which costs real time at scale. The documented fix for exactly this scenario is `virtualStoreType: global` in `pnpm-workspace.yaml` (or `.npmrc`): every worktree's `node_modules` becomes pure symlinks into one shared global store, "near-zero per-worktree overhead." **Set this explicitly for `second-brain-claudekit`, don't rely on pnpm's default per-project behavior.**
- **`internship-research-loop` (uv):** uv's global content-addressed cache uses hardlinks/copy-on-write by default (confirmed, `docs.astral.sh/uv/concepts/cache`) — no extra setting needed, but it only works when the cache and the `.venv` share a filesystem; if they're on different volumes, uv silently falls back to slow copies with no warning. **Verification step for Phase 1:** confirm `uv cache dir` and every worktree's path under `~/projects` resolve to the same filesystem (`df` both paths, compare the device) — this is already satisfied by Prompt 2's own "never work from `/mnt/c` or `/mnt/d`" rule, but confirm it rather than inferring it.

## Decision 8 — Tailscale key/node expiry: the one-click fix, not just a known risk
Confirmed directly (`tailscale.com/docs/features/access-control/auth-keys`): node key expiry defaults to a maximum of 90 days (sources elsewhere cite 180 days for some expiry policies — the admin console's actual per-node value is the one to trust, not either number from memory). A device whose key silently expires looks completely fine for weeks, then Remote-SSH simply stops connecting with no useful local error.
**Concrete fix, to run once during Phase 4 setup, not left as an ongoing risk to remember:** in the Tailscale admin console → Device Management → find the Dell's `dell-wsl` node → **Disable key expiry** for that specific node. This is a real, documented, permanent setting, not a recurring maintenance task.

## Decision 9 — Tunnels fallback: set it up and test it now
VS Code's Remote Tunnels (confirmed, `code.visualstudio.com/docs/remote/tunnels`) is a real, separate mechanism from Remote-SSH — it tunnels through a Microsoft/GitHub account as the broker, works through any firewall, and needs no SSH at all. Its one hard constraint, stated directly in Microsoft's own docs: **the remote machine is only reachable through the tunnel while VS Code (or the `code tunnel` CLI) is actually running there.** For this to be a real fallback rather than a hopeful one, confirm during Phase 4 (not during an actual Dell-unreachable emergency) whether a persistent, logon-independent tunnel service can be installed (`code tunnel service install` or equivalent) rather than relying on an interactively-opened VS Code window that closes the tunnel the moment it exits.

## Decision 10 — this is not a devcontainer, and nothing here changes that
No new research changed this from the prior brief: Remote-SSH runs VS Code's backend on one specific physical machine; nothing here is portable or reproducible across hosts the way a devcontainer definition would be. If reproducibility across unrelated machines is ever wanted later, that's an additive, separate project — not a reason to delay this one.

## Decision 11 — concurrent-session model: one filesystem, not a sync race; separation is by task, not by physical machine

> [!NOTE] Added 2026-10-14, from a planning-session Q&A, not yet exercised live
> This decision answers "what actually happens if the Dell and the Acer touch the same checkout at once" and "where does global config/conversation state land" — both came up as open questions and have a precise mechanical answer, not a "depends."

Under the single-canonical-host design there is **one filesystem** for everything under `~/projects` on the Dell. A Remote-SSH session driven from the Acer is not a second copy racing the Dell's — it is a second live process pointed at the exact same files. This is a narrower, more dangerous failure mode than the vaults' own Syncthing sync-conflict mechanic (see the case study below): a plain concurrent save on the same checkout has **no conflict-copy safety net at all**, it just silently overwrites.

- **Worktree separation is by concurrently-active task, not by which physical machine is driving.** Decision 4's `<machine>/<topic>` branch naming is attribution, not the actual safety mechanism — the mechanism is that each concurrently-active session gets its own `git worktree add` directory, so two editors never share an inode. If a Dell-native session and an Acer-driven Remote-SSH session are ever open on the same branch/checkout path at the same time, that is the violation, regardless of whose name is on the branch.
- **No automated lock enforces "one session owns a branch at a time."** This is a discipline rule, not software — nothing warns you when it's violated. The planned `~/tools/sync-check` (SSH Runbook step 4.9) reports branch/ahead-behind/dirty state, not "is a live session currently open elsewhere" — that gap stays open.
- **Global Claude Code config (`~/.claude/settings.json`, `~/.claude.json`, skills, memory) follows the execution host, not the human's physical seat.** A Remote-SSH session into `dell-wsl` always reads the Dell's global config, never the Acer's, because the `claude`/`codex` process and the Remote-SSH workspace-extension host both run on the Dell. Build 2 item 5 already closed the one safety-critical gap this caused (Acer's secret-file deny-rules were missing from the Dell's `~/.claude/settings.json`; now applied). Any other Acer-only global skill/setting still will not appear on the Dell — nothing syncs this going forward, by the same one-way-visibility-only logic as the `.claude`-mirror decision elsewhere in this vault.
- **Conversation transcripts land wherever the process ran, not wherever the human sat.** A Claude Code session driven from the Acer via Remote-SSH writes its transcript to the Dell's `~/.claude/projects/...`, same as a native Dell session. This should mean the existing WSL Claude Code capture pipeline (see memory `project_ai_conversation_archive.md`) already covers Remote-SSH-driven sessions — not yet live-verified for that specific trigger path, so confirm rather than assume. Acer-only local work (never touching `~/projects` on the Dell) needs the Acer's own separate capture pipeline, which is a distinct, unverified item.

**Real-world precedent for the "no safety net" risk, from this vault's own history, not hypothetical:** two Dell-side Claude Code sessions editing the Old Laptop Rebuild notes concurrently on 2026-10-13 produced 15 Syncthing `.sync-conflict-*` files (see `Runs\_backups\sync-conflicts-20261013\`). No content was lost there only because Syncthing was watching those files and wrote conflict copies — the exact protection a plain code checkout under this design does not have. The Index's own response was procedural ("one owner per note" — only the session closing a build edits the Index/Runs Index) rather than technical, because no technical lock exists. The same discipline-not-software gap applies to code worktrees.

## What Prompt 1 (`.wslconfig` owner) must add
- `[wsl2] vmIdleTimeout=-1` and `[general] instanceIdleTimeout=-1` — currently absent from its target-state list entirely (Decision 3).
- A live verification step: close all WSL sessions, wait 30s+, confirm the distro is still `Running` and sshd still answers, before Prompt 2's Phase 4 is allowed to treat the host as reliably reachable.

## What Prompt 2 (two-laptop workflow owner) must change
- Flip the primary/fallback order: Tailscale SSH primary, keyed OpenSSH fallback (Decision 2) — keep the already-planned hardening file, but note it's fallback-only.
- Add the explicit "Tailscale: WSL only, never Windows, on either laptop" standing rule (Decision 1), plus a live MTU/round-trip test under the Dell's actual `networkingMode=mirrored`.
- Add a Phase 4 step to inventory and retire/relabel the Acer's existing clones of both repos before the handoff completes (Decision 4).
- Add a step to patch `internship-research-loop-new-laptop-directive.md`'s completion gate with a superseded-by note (Decision 5) — leave `second-brain-claudekit`'s gate untouched.
- Add one explicit out-of-scope line for the Jarvis/The Plan vaults (Decision 6).
- Add the pnpm `virtualStoreType: global` setting for `second-brain-claudekit` and the uv same-filesystem verification for `internship-research-loop` (Decision 7).
- Add a Phase 4 step to disable key expiry on the `dell-wsl` node in the Tailscale admin console (Decision 8), and to test a persistent Remote Tunnels fallback before it's ever actually needed (Decision 9).
- Resolve, don't assert, whether a default personal-tailnet ACL already permits Tailscale SSH or needs an explicit `ssh` policy block — check the admin console directly during Phase 4 (Decision 2's open item).

## Sources (checked live, 2026-10-04)
- `tailscale.com/docs/features/tailscale-ssh` — authentication model, netstack interception, host-key distribution, OS-user limitation.
- `tailscale.com/docs/install/windows/wsl2` (last validated 2025-11-03) — the both-at-once MTU/double-encapsulation warning, the 1280→1340 MTU workaround.
- `tailscale.com/docs/features/access-control/auth-keys` — 90-day default key expiry, admin-console override.
- `learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/wsl/wsl-config` — `vmIdleTimeout` (60000ms default) vs `instanceIdleTimeout` (15000ms default), the two-setting fix.
- GitHub `microsoft/WSL` discussion #9245 (dated comment, April 2026) — live confirmation that `vmIdleTimeout=-1` alone does not prevent instance shutdown.
- `pnpm.io/git-worktrees`, `pnpm.io/global-virtual-store` — `virtualStoreType: global` for near-zero per-worktree `node_modules` cost.
- `docs.astral.sh/uv/concepts/cache` — uv's hardlink/copy-on-write global cache and its same-filesystem requirement.
- `code.visualstudio.com/docs/remote/tunnels` — Remote Tunnels' VS-Code-must-be-running constraint.

# How to — this repo's conversation-capture pipeline, real current state

`_docs/Jarvis.md` and `_docs/Gaps.md` (both dated 2026-08-19) describe capture as stalled — nothing recorded on WSL since 2026-08-12 or Windows since 2026-08-10, root cause diagnosed as `SessionEnd` not firing reliably with no scheduled backfill safety net. Per this session's own instruction, that snapshot was re-verified against the live Jarvis vault rather than assumed — it does not describe the state as of right now.

## Re-verified 2026-08-19, directly against the Jarvis vault

**A second, distinct bug was found and fixed today, in a parallel session, separate from this one:** `60_Claude/05_Clippings/AI Conversations/WSL/Claude Code/second-brain-claudekit/08-19 Stop hook errors with System.Runtime.Numerics.md` (WSL, 05:05–05:56 today) is the real transcript. Root cause: every `pwsh`-invoked Stop/SessionEnd hook was crashing with `System.IO.FileLoadException: The given assembly name was invalid. File name: 'System.Runtime.Numerics...'` — a .NET assembly-load error inside `pwsh` itself, not a bug in the hook scripts' own logic. Confirmed reproduced directly (`pwsh -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File .claude/hooks/session-wrapup.ps1` crashing with the same trace), and confirmed the hook's actual output (the real reminder text, real `wsl-session-export.ps1` side effects) was still produced on stdout despite the crash noise on stderr.

**Fix, applied at the settings.json command layer, not inside the `.ps1` files** — matches this session's own memory of this exact class of bug: every Stop/SessionEnd hook command in both `/home/anant_gupta/.claude/settings.json` (global) and this repo's `.claude/settings.json` (project) was changed from `pwsh -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File <script>` to `pwsh -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File <script> 2>/dev/null; exit 0` — suppressing the crash's stderr noise and forcing exit 0 so Claude Code never sees a hook failure, while the script's real stdout output and real side effects (writing the export, writing the reminder) still happen. Verified end-to-end: JSON syntax valid on both files after the edit, and a simulated concurrent 3-hook run (matching the real "Ran 3 stop hooks" scenario from the bug report) completed with no errors surfacing.

**Real, post-fix captures exist, dated today:**
- WSL: `.../WSL/Claude Code/second-brain-claudekit/08-19 Review codebase structure and document architecture decisions.md` and `08-19 Qualification pipeline structural base.md` — both real, substantial (170KB+) session exports.
- Windows: `.../Windows/Claude Code/Jarvis/08-19 Reply with exactly hook wiring test ok..md` — a real, minimal test capture (`hook wiring test ok.`), exported cleanly at `2026-08-19T11:08:10`, confirming the Windows-side export hook is also live today.

**Conclusion: the pwsh-crash bug that was silently killing every Stop/SessionEnd hook is fixed and verified working today, on both WSL and Windows.** This is a different, narrower bug than the one `_docs/Jarvis.md`/`_docs/Gaps.md` diagnosed (`SessionEnd` not firing for every termination path, no scheduled backfill) — fixing this one restores normal per-session capture; it does not by itself add the scheduled backfill safety net for termination paths that never fire `SessionEnd` at all (an abrupt terminal close, machine sleep, WSL shutdown mid-session).

## The scheduled-backfill safety net — also resolved today, in the parallel session

`60_Claude/05_Clippings/AI Conversations/Windows/Claude Code/Jarvis/08-19 Fix Claude Code conversation-capture scheduled task reliability.md` is that parallel session's real transcript, targeting the deeper gap `_docs/Gaps.md` named as "the single most consequential open gap": `SessionEnd` not firing for every real termination path, with no scheduled backfill to catch what it misses.

Re-verified directly against the live vault, not taken on the other session's word alone: `60_Claude/05_Clippings/AI Conversations/00 - Capture Health.md` exists — an auto-generated dashboard, "Do not edit by hand — edits are overwritten," written by `update-capture-health.ps1` on every backfill run. Its real content, read directly today:

- **Windows backfill:** last run `2026-08-19T07:11:36Z`, exit 0 (OK), no current failure streak — 6 consecutive OK runs shown, roughly every 10-20 minutes.
- **WSL backfill:** last run `2026-08-19T07:15:41Z`, exit 0 (OK), no current failure streak — 4 consecutive OK runs shown.

Per that session's own summary (`_docs/Gaps.md`'s citation): native Windows Task Scheduler retry (`RestartCount=3`/`RestartInterval=PT2M`) was enabled on both backfill tasks, the TaskScheduler Operational event log was enabled, Windows Defender exclusions were applied (a plausible silent-failure cause on Windows), and a related gap — the global Windows `settings.json` missing the `jarvis-session-continuity.ps1` hook — was closed and verified with a real headless test session.

**Both halves of the capture pipeline — per-session Stop/SessionEnd export, and the scheduled backfill safety net for terminations that skip SessionEnd entirely — are confirmed fixed and running as of 2026-08-19,** verified by this session directly against real, timestamped, auto-generated dashboard data, not assumed from either session's own narrative.

## What this means for this repo

Nothing in this repo's own files needed to change to fix the pwsh-crash bug — the fix lives entirely in Jarvis-side / global hook config (`~/.claude/settings.json`, this repo's own `.claude/settings.json`), which is why `_docs/Gaps.md` correctly notes "this isn't a `60_Claude/` file problem directly." This doc exists so a future session reading `_docs/Gaps.md`'s 2026-08-19 snapshot doesn't assume that snapshot is still current — re-verify against the live vault (`mcp__jarvis__vault_list` on the relevant `AI Conversations` subfolder) rather than trusting either this doc or `_docs/Gaps.md` indefinitely.

# Conversation Capture

Capture an LLM conversation so its decisions survive after the chat closes. This is the biggest missing layer in Jarvis and the backbone of the three-month plan: you make many decisions with AI tools, and without capture, every new AI starts from zero.

**Use when:** a conversation with any AI (Claude, Codex, Cursor, Kiro, ChatGPT, a local model) produced a decision, a plan, or context worth keeping.

**Moves:** raw transcript → `60_Claude/05_Clippings/AI Conversations/` → summary in `60_Claude/07_AI_Information/AI Conversation - Summaries/` (and the `jarvis-memory` registry under `30_Order/System/` indexes both).

**Serves Standard:** [[Conversations]]. **Template:** [[AI Conversation]].

## The three layers

Raw conversation is not knowledge yet. Store it in three layers and only promote the durable parts:

1. **Raw archive** — the transcript, untouched, in `05_Clippings/AI Conversations/`.
2. **Summary** — a short distillation in `AI Conversation - Summaries/`, shaped by the [[AI Conversation]] template and the [[Conversations]] Standard.
3. **Promotion** — only durable decisions become concept notes, project updates, outputs, or `next:` actions. The transcript itself never becomes a durable note.

## Steps

1. Save the raw transcript to `05_Clippings/AI Conversations/`. Do not edit it.
2. Write the summary in `AI Conversation - Summaries/` from the [[AI Conversation]] template. Keep it short: the value is the decisions, not the back-and-forth.
3. Wikilink the project and concept notes the conversation touched.
4. Promote only what is durable: turn a decision into a project `next:`, a new understanding into a distilled note, an artifact into an output. Leave the rest as summary.
5. Append a one-line session-log entry noting the import and whether anything was promoted.

## Frontmatter

Comes from the [[AI Conversation]] template. Do not write it from memory.

## Done when

- The raw transcript is archived and untouched.
- A short summary captures decisions, changes, and open questions.
- Durable items are promoted; the transcript is not promoted wholesale.
- The session log records the import.

# AI Conversations — Raw Archive
This folder is the raw archive for exported LLM conversation transcripts (Claude Code, Claude web, Codex, Cursor, Kiro, ChatGPT, Ollama, future tools).
## Structure (added 2026-07-05)
Split by OS environment first, then by tool, since Claude Code/Kiro/Cursor each maintain separate config and session history on Windows vs WSL:
```
AI Conversations/
  Windows/
    Claude Code/   ← wired up (2026-07-31 rebuild): per-project subfolders, ai-title titling, rich tool-call capture, fully automatic via SessionEnd hook — see below
    Cowork/        ← wired up (2026-07-31 rebuild): per-month subfolders, swept from %APPDATA%\Claude\local-agent-mode-sessions on every session end — see below
    Kiro/          ← scaffold only, not wired
    Cursor/        ← wired up (2026-07-30): per-project subfolders, JSONL+SQLite join, Task Scheduler sweep — see below
  WSL/
    Claude Code/   ← wired up (2026-07-30): per-project subfolders, raw JSONL mirror + richer exported markdown notes (tool calls captured, not just names), fully automatic via SessionEnd hook — see below
    Kiro/          ← scaffold only, not wired
    Cursor/        ← wired up (2026-07-30): same two-source join as Windows/Cursor, routed here for vscode-remote / file://wsl$ workspaces
  Claude App/      ← Desktop app, cloud-based — not part of the Windows/WSL split
  OpenCode/
```
`Windows/Claude Code/` (rebuilt 2026-07-31, mirroring the WSL fix) exports **every** Windows Claude Code project unconditionally — the old exporter's `$isJarvis` cwd-allowlist silently dropped every session outside the
```
Windows/Claude Code/<ProjectName>/
  MM-DD {title}.md          ← one per session (folder already says "Claude Code", filename doesn't repeat it)
  _raw_jsonl/                ← NTFS junction straight to that project's whole ~/.claude/projects/<hash> folder — live, not a point-in-time copy, since Windows can junction locally (no WSL/DrvFs boundary to work aroun
  .exported/                 ← per-session marker files (<session_id>.done) — the dedup mechanism, not a shared index
  _archive-pre-fix/           ← pre-2026-07-31 flat notes, archived not deleted
  00 - Session Index.md      ← Dataview query over this folder
  00 - Tool Usage Rollup.md  ← DataviewJS tool/token/cost/file aggregates
```
Titles come from the last `ai-title` JSONL entry when present; Claude Code sessions predating that feature (and Cowork transcripts, which never emit it — see below) fall back to a slug of the first real human turn, exp

`Windows/Cowork/` covers Claude Desktop's Cowork/Agent-Mode feature, which spins up a fully isolated `.claude` sandbox per task under `%APPDATA%\Claude\local-agent-mode-sessions`. Cowork transcripts have no stable `cwd`/

Both exporters share one script, `30_Order/System/claude-workflow/scripts/export-claude-session.ps1` (`-BackfillAll` for a full walk, or single-session args from the `SessionEnd` hook — `30_Order/System/claude-workflow

`Windows/Cursor/` and `WSL/Cursor/` (rewired 2026-07-30) join **two** stores — the old SQLite-only / on-demand `_raw_composer/` story is retired:

1. **Per-session JSONL** under `~/.cursor/projects/**/agent-transcripts/<uuid>/<uuid>.jsonl` — lives on the OS where the workspace ran (WSL home or Windows profile). Shape is lean: `role` + `message.content[]` with `te
2. **Windows-only SQLite** `%APPDATA%\Cursor\User\globalStorage\state.vscdb`, table `composerHeaders` — `composerId` equals the JSONL filename. The `value` JSON supplies `name` (title), `subtitle`, `createdAt` / `lastU

Routing uses `workspaceIdentifier.uri` (fallback: `workspaceStorage/<workspaceId>/workspace.json`): `vscode-remote://wsl+...` or `file://wsl$/...` → `WSL/Cursor/<project>/`; `file:///c:/...` / `file:///d:/...` → `Win

Layout per project (mirrors WSL Claude Code):
```
{WSL|Windows}/Cursor/<project>/
  MM-DD {name}.md                 ← one per session
  _raw_jsonl/                     ← Windows: NTFS junction to that Cursor project's agent-transcripts/; WSL: one-way per-session .jsonl copy (DrvFs can't junction)
  _archive-pre-fix/               ← pre-2026-07-30 flat notes, archived not deleted

    Set-Content -LiteralPath $rollupPath -Value $roll -Encoding UTF8

function Export-Session {
    param(
        [string]$TranscriptPath,
        [string]$Cwd,
        [string]$SessionId,
        [string]$ExportedAt
    )

    $result = [pscustomobject]@{ Status = "error"; Project = $null }

    if (-not $Cwd -or -not $SessionId -or -not $TranscriptPath) { return $result }
    if (-not (Test-Path -LiteralPath $TranscriptPath)) { return $result }
    if (-not (Test-Path -LiteralPath $VaultConvRoot)) { return $result }

    $projectName = Split-Path -Path ($Cwd.TrimEnd('/')) -Leaf
    if (-not $projectName) { $projectName = "unknown" }
    $result.Project = $projectName

    $projectDir = Join-Path $VaultConvRoot $projectName
    $rawDir = Join-Path $projectDir "_raw_jsonl"
    New-Item -ItemType Directory -Path $rawDir -Force | Out-Null
    $markerDir = Join-Path $projectDir ".exported"
    New-Item -ItemType Directory -Path $markerDir -Force | Out-Null

## Claude

Login expired · Please run /login

I'm doing groundwork first, so the prompt is exact and not guessed. That means fetching the Sonnet 5.5 prompting guide you linked, and reading the capture scripts the session will change, plus the live state of the repos it will remove. All of it is read-only.

Last groundwork checks: that `pwsh` actually runs on the Acer WSL (the exporter needs it), what the capture-health script expects, and how the vault ignores `_raw_jsonl`.

Everything the session needs is verified, and I'm leaving nothing for it to guess. Some of what I found changes the plan:

- **Dell Windows has no `SessionEnd` hook.** It captures only through the `ConversationCapture-Backfill-Windows` task.
- **The Dell's WSL hook is hard-coded to a folder that no longer exists.** That is why capture there has produced nothing since the vault was split.
- **The `Dell/` archive was made by renaming folders.** Its dedup markers were kept, so the new exporter must honour the old markers or it will re-export hundreds of sessions as duplicates.
- **The Capture Health note is per-machine,** because it is ignored by Git and Syncthing, so machines don't collide there.
- **`gpu-smoke-test` has no remote.** It is about 100 KB of source plus a 5.3 GB `.venv`, so the session must not delete it blindly.

## How to run it
In Acer WSL: `cd ~ && claude`, then `/model` set to Sonnet 5.5, and effort `high`. The guide says to use `medium` for well-specified work and `high` for longer or harder work. This one has five workstreams, so I used `high`. Stay at the keyboard for one Tailscale confirmation and possibly one elevation prompt. Don't edit Jarvis notes on the Dell while it runs.

<rules>

A syntax-only check, or a check that failed to start, does not count. If no real check can run here (for example a reboot test or a GUI window), say which one you did not run and why.
- Before you rely on anything that may have changed since your training, check current documentation or search, even when you feel confident: the Tailscale Windows "run unattended" option and its CLI flag, the VS Code Remote-SSH setting names and scopes, the Windows Terminal profile schema, the "code --remote" syntax, systemd timer options, and "gh pr merge" flags.
- Back up every file before editing it, to a dated copy beside it or under ~/.config/.toolkit-backups/, and read it back after. Quarantine or bundle anything that is not a regenerable cache before removing it. Never delete ~/.cache/node (it holds the Corepack pnpm pin).
- Never print secrets: no ~/.mcp.env, tokens, keys, ssh private keys, auth files, env dumps, or transcript bodies. Names, counts, sizes and paths are enough.
- Never run wsl --shutdown or wsl --terminate, never reboot, and never restart a service on the Dell other than the one user timer you create there. If a step needs elevation, give me the exact command and wait.
- Tailscale: the only change allowed is the unattended option on the Acer. Do not run tailscale up, down, logout or set anything else, and do not touch ACLs or the Dell node.
- Reaching the Dell: use ssh to 100.94.129.113 as anant_gupta with a temporary known_hosts that holds only the key you obtained by ssh-keyscan, after confirming its fingerprint equals SHA256:[REDACTED], with StrictHostKeyChecking=yes. Never edit a real known_hosts. If a command prints a login.tailscale.com URL (Tailscale SSH check mode), stop and ask me to open it, then continue; do not retry in a loop. On the Dell you may only: read, edit ~/.claude/settings.json (export entries only), replace the export hook, and create one systemd user timer and service.
- Windows PowerShell 5.1 started from WSL inherits PowerShell 7's PSModulePath and then fails with "Get-FileHash not recognized". Start every Windows script with: $env:PSModulePath = [Environment]::GetEnvironmentVariable('PSModulePath','Machine'). Run Windows scripts as files with -File. Scripts you write: ASCII only, safe to run twice, dry run by default, a -Apply switch, a log, no list-typed command-line parameters, and no 2>$null around native commands when ErrorActionPreference is Stop. Edit JSONC files (VS Code settings) as text so comments survive.
- Vault writes follow $V/AGENTS.md: nothing at the vault root, keep frontmatter, patch by heading, update "updated:", no em dashes anywhere, back up each edited note to Old Laptop Rebuild/Runs/_backups/acer-5c-20261017/. You own the Index and Runs Index for this run. If Syncthing creates a *.sync-conflict-* file next to a note you edited, diff it, merge anything unique, then move it to Runs/_backups/. Bulk exporter output also travels through Syncthing: after a backfill, wait until it is idle and confirm no new conflict files.
- If a step fails twice for the same reason, stop and report the cause and the smallest next check. If the live state contradicts these facts, trust the machine and log the difference.
- Before your first tool call say what you are starting. After each phase give a two-line recap and the next step.

## Notes on the prompt
- **Why 2d asks you:** a blind delete of `gpu-smoke-test` would lose your only GPU validation project, since it has no remote. The prompt makes the session ask once near the end.
- **One catch worth knowing about:** the shared Windows exporter is used by the Dell's Windows backfill task too. Editing it changes where the Dell's own Windows captures land from its next run, which is why the prompt requires the legacy-marker check and a dry run before any real backfill.
- **Cursor routing:** I left Cursor routing out of this build on purpose and listed it as an open item. Dell reboot resilience is the next build, as you asked.

**Tool calls:**

- `Agent` — `{"description":"Fetch Sonnet 5.5 prompting guide","subagent_type":"web-fetch","prompt":"Fetch https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/build-with-claude/prompt-engineering/[REDACTED] and report back, as the answer to this task, a faithful and fairly complete summary of what the page recommends for writing prompts for Claude Sonnet 5.5, especially for long-running agentic coding and infrastructure tasks. Quote key short phrases where useful. Do not summarize beyond what the page says."}`

######## export-claude-session.ps1: params, outputs, markers, modes
807 /mnt/d/_Anant/20_Progress/Documents/Jarvis/30_Order/System/claude-workflow/scripts/export-claude-session.ps1
param(
    [string]$TranscriptPath = "",
    [string]$SessionId = "",
    [string]$Cwd = "",
    [ValidateSet("ClaudeCode", "Cowork")]
    [string]$SourceApp = "ClaudeCode",
    [switch]$BackfillAll
)

$ErrorActionPreference = "Stop"

$VaultRoot = (Resolve-Path (Join-Path $PSScriptRoot "..\..\..\..")).Path
$OutRoot = Join-Path $VaultRoot "60_Claude\05_Clippings\AI Conversations\Windows"

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Pricing (dollars per million tokens). Reused from the WSL exporter's table,
# looked up 2026-07-30 via the claude-api skill - one day old as of this
# script's authorship (2026-07-31), so treated as current rather than
# re-derived. Sonnet 5 intro pricing ($2/$10) runs through 2026-08-31, then
# reverts to $3/$15 - revisit this table after that date.
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
$Pricing = @{
    "claude-fable-5"    = @{ input = 10.00; output = 50.00 }
    "claude-mythos-5"   = @{ input = 10.00; output = 50.00 }
    "claude-opus-5"     = @{ input = 5.00; output = 25.00 }
    "claude-opus-4-8"   = @{ input = 5.00; output = 25.00 }
    "claude-opus-4-7"   = @{ input = 5.00; output = 25.00 }
    "claude-opus-4-6"   = @{ input = 5.00; output = 25.00 }
    "claude-sonnet-5"   = @{ input = 2.00; output = 10.00 }
    "claude-sonnet-4-6" = @{ input = 3.00; output = 15.00 }
    "claude-haiku-4-5"  = @{ input = 1.00; output = 5.00 }
}

function Resolve-PricingKey {
    param([string]$Model)
    if (-not $Model -or $Model -eq '<synthetic>') { return $null }
    $key = $Model -replace '-\d{8}$', '' -replace '\[1m\]$', ''
    if ($Pricing.ContainsKey($key)) { return $key }
    return $null
}

######## vbs launchers
' Silent launcher for ConversationCapture-Backfill-WSL.
' Same corrected hidden + synchronous + honest-exit-code pattern as
' sync-all-silent.vbs (2026-08-11). Safety-net catch-up for WSL Claude Code
' sessions the SessionEnd/Stop hooks missed - not the primary mechanism.
Option Explicit
Dim sh, exitCode, scriptDir, healthScript
Set sh = CreateObject("WScript.Shell")
scriptDir = CreateObject("Scripting.FileSystemObject").GetParentFolderName(WScript.ScriptFullName)
healthScript = scriptDir & "\update-capture-health.ps1"
exitCode = sh.Run("wsl.exe -e bash -lc ""pwsh -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File ~/.claude/hooks/wsl-session-export.ps1 -BackfillAll""", 0, True)
' Records this run's honest exit code to the Capture Health dashboard note
' (added 2026-08-19 - reliability gap fix, see the CoreCLR crash-in-pwsh
' investigation). Runs after the real work so it can never mask exitCode
' below; ignore its own exit status.
sh.Run "powershell -NoProfile -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File """ & healthScript & """ -TaskLabel WSL -ExitCode " & exitCode, 0, True
WScript.Quit(exitCode)
----
' Silent launcher for ConversationCapture-Backfill-Windows.
' WindowStyle 0 = hidden. Synchronous wait (waitOnReturn=True) + honest exit
' code, same corrected pattern as sync-all-silent.vbs (2026-08-11). This is
' a safety-net catch-up for anything the SessionEnd/Stop hooks missed - the
' hooks are the primary capture mechanism, this just closes the gap when a
' hook doesn't fire (crash, forced close, machine sleep mid-session).
Option Explicit
Dim sh, exitCode, scriptDir, exportScript, healthScript
Set sh = CreateObject("WScript.Shell")
scriptDir = CreateObject("Scripting.FileSystemObject").GetParentFolderName(WScript.ScriptFullName)
exportScript = scriptDir & "\export-claude-session.ps1"
healthScript = scriptDir & "\update-capture-health.ps1"
exitCode = sh.Run("powershell -NoProfile -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File """ & exportScript & """ -BackfillAll", 0, True)
' Records this run's honest exit code to the Capture Health dashboard note
' (added 2026-08-19 - reliability gap fix). Runs after the real work so it
' can never mask exitCode below; ignore its own exit status.
sh.Run "powershell -NoProfile -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File """ & healthScript & """ -TaskLabel Windows -ExitCode " & exitCode, 0, True
WScript.Quit(exitCode)

######## DELL WSL exporter: params and invocation

param(
    [switch]$BackfillAll
)

$ErrorActionPreference = "Stop"

$VaultConvRoot = "/mnt/d/Users/_Anant/10_Areas/Documents/Jarvis/60_Claude/05_Clippings/AI Conversations/WSL/Claude Code"

$Pricing = @{
    "claude-fable-5"    = @{ input = 10.00; output = 50.00 }
    "claude-mythos-5"   = @{ input = 10.00; output = 50.00 }
    "claude-opus-5"     = @{ input = 5.00; output = 25.00 }
    "claude-opus-4-8"   = @{ input = 5.00; output = 25.00 }
    "claude-opus-4-7"   = @{ input = 5.00; output = 25.00 }
    "claude-opus-4-6"   = @{ input = 5.00; output = 25.00 }
    "claude-sonnet-5"   = @{ input = 2.00; output = 10.00 }
    "claude-sonnet-4-6" = @{ input = 3.00; output = 15.00 }
    "claude-haiku-4-5"  = @{ input = 1.00; output = 5.00 }
}

function Resolve-PricingKey {
...
                continue
            }
            if ($r.Project) {
                $touchedProjects[$r.Project] = $true
                if (-not $counts.ContainsKey($r.Project)) {
                    $counts[$r.Project] = @{ seen = 0; written = 0; junk = 0; dup = 0 }
                }
                $counts[$r.Project].seen++
                switch ($r.Status) {
                    "written" { $counts[$r.Project].written++ }
                    "junk"    { $counts[$r.Project].junk++ }
                    "dup"     { $counts[$r.Project].dup++ }
                }
            }
        }

        foreach ($p in $touchedProjects.Keys) {
            $projectDir = Join-Path $VaultConvRoot $p
            try { Update-Rollups -ProjectDir $projectDir -ProjectName $p } catch {}
        }

        Write-Output "=== Backfill summary ==="
        foreach ($p in ($counts.Keys | Sort-Object)) {
            $c = $counts[$p]
            Write-Output "$p : seen=$($c.seen) written=$($c.written) junk=$($c.junk) dup=$($c.dup)"
        }
    }
    else {
        $rawInput = [Console]::In.ReadToEnd()
        if ([string]::IsNullOrWhiteSpace($rawInput)) { exit 0 }

        $hookInput = $null
        try { $hookInput = $rawInput | ConvertFrom-Json } catch { exit 0 }
        if (-not $hookInput) { exit 0 }
        $eventName = [string]$hookInput.hook_event_name
        if ($eventName -ne "SessionEnd" -and $eventName -ne "Stop") { exit 0 }

        $cwd = [string]$hookInput.cwd
        $sessionId = [string]$hookInput.session_id
        $transcriptPath = [string]$hookInput.transcript_path
        # Plain ISO, no fractional seconds/offset - matches started_at/ended_at
        # and Windows/Claude Code's 2026-07-31 rebuild, which normalized this
        # exact inconsistency (WSL previously used the round-trip "o" format
        # for this one key only).
        $exportedAt = (Get-Date).ToString("yyyy-MM-ddTHH:mm:ss")

        $r = Export-Session -TranscriptPath $transcriptPath -Cwd $cwd -SessionId $sessionId -ExportedAt $exportedAt
        if ($r.Project) {
            try {
                $projectDir = Join-Path $VaultConvRoot $r.Project
                Update-Rollups -ProjectDir $projectDir -ProjectName $r.Project
            } catch {}
        }
    }
} catch {
    # fail open - never block session exit on a bad or half-written transcript
}

exit 0

25:Every conflict-file incident this sync setup has had, across Builds 1-13, traces to one of a handful of patterns: a platform/type mismatch Syncthing can't materialize identically on both machines, a secret that nearly or actually left the machine, a safety-net setting that silently reverted or got disabled without anyone noticing, two independent write mechanisms touching the same file without 
56:**Fix (2026-09-28):** `git-auto-sync.ps1` patched to check Syncthing's own live `db/status` before touching the working tree (skips the run if not `idle`/`needBytes: 0`/`errors: 0`, deferring to the next tick), and to pause this machine's Syncthing folder for the exact duration of its own pull/rebase/commit/push, resuming in a `finally` block. This script lives inside the synced vault, so the f
57:**Prevention:** the debounce window (`fsWatcherDelayS`) narrows the race; the pause/resume coordination in the script closes it structurally, on whichever machine is actually running the patched script with a live Syncthing instance to talk to. Both machines need the same watcher-delay and versioning settings — this is a standing gap in the design (per-machine `config.xml` isn't and shouldn't
68:**Exercised again, 2026-10-09 (Dell), the worst git-side outage so far:** `git-auto-sync` ended `FAILED, pull --rebase conflict` on every run from 20:48 onward (30 consecutive failures by 23:03) and the Dell fell 34 commits behind origin. Cause: the script's recovery for "untracked file collides with an incoming commit" commits the colliding files first (`Auto-sync: pre-pull commit...`). While 
114:**Fix:** all 14 added to `.gitignore` and `git rm --cached` (kept on disk, untracked going forward). `weekly-review.log` got the same treatment pre-emptively - same per-machine-writer shape as `git-auto-sync.log` (Build 5), added to both lists for the first time.
123:**Prevention:** a Task Scheduler registration (and, more generally, any one-time `Register-*`/`New-*` system-state call a setup script makes) is itself per-machine state, same category as [[Cross-Laptop Sync - Operations Reference]] Part 7's config.xml warning. **A script fix that changes what a registration *would* produce needs a deliberate re-run of that registration on every machine** - it
124:**Re-verified live, 2026-10-07 (Acer):** the Acer's own `Jarvis-WeeklyReview` trigger confirmed correct (`SUN`/`06:00:00`). This entry is specifically about per-machine drift, so the Acer being correct says nothing about the Dell - **not checked, cannot be checked from this machine.** Run `schtasks /query /tn "Jarvis-WeeklyReview" /fo LIST /v` on the Dell directly to confirm.
128:**Fixed (Build 11):** the live, divergent banner text moved into a new per-machine file, `30_Order/System/sync-workflow/Sync Alert Banner.md`, excluded from both `.gitignore` and `.stignore` (same shape as `.sync-alert-state.json`). `00_Dashboard.md` now holds exactly one permanent line, `![[30_Order/System/sync-workflow/Sync Alert Banner]]`, inside the same `BEGIN`/`END` markers - an Obsidian

7.6.6
== update-capture-health.ps1 params
# Records one backfill-task run result and regenerates the capture-health
# dashboard note. Called by backfill-windows-silent.vbs and
# backfill-wsl-silent.vbs with the exit code they already capture from
# sh.Run - this does not re-run or wrap the backfill itself, so a native
# crash in the backfill process (see 2026-08-19 CoreCLR crash investigation)
# still shows up here even though it can't be caught inside the backfill
# script's own try/catch.
#
# No LLM/API calls - plain state-file + Set-Content only, safe to run on
# every single backfill invocation (every 30 min x2 tasks).

param(
    [Parameter(Mandatory = $true)]
    [ValidateSet("Windows", "WSL")]
    [string]$TaskLabel,

    [Parameter(Mandatory = $true)]
    [int]$ExitCode
)

$ErrorActionPreference = "Stop"

$repoRoot = (Resolve-Path (Join-Path $PSScriptRoot "..\..\..\..")).Path
$logsDir = Join-Path $repoRoot "30_Order\System\claude-workflow\logs"
$notePath = Join-Path $repoRoot "60_Claude\05_Clippings\AI Conversations\00 - Capture Health.md"
